*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
*comment SHARED SMALL-COMBAT ENGINE
*comment All dice math (attack rolls, damage, spell slots, temp HP, locks)
*comment lives in startup.txt -- this file just wires those primitives into
*comment a playable round-by-round loop and owns the mechanical shell every
*comment fight shares (the choice skeleton, the enemy-turn resolution). Every static narration text pool this
*comment file selects from (weapon hits/misses/finishers, enemy type flavor, spell flavor) lives in
*comment combat_prose.txt instead (split out 2026-09-28 to keep this file mechanics-focused) -- reached with
*comment *gosub_scene combat_prose <label>, never a bare *gosub, same as every other cross-file call in this
*comment codebase. See combat_prose.txt's own header for the full list and the calling convention.
*comment
*comment Calling convention: a scene starts a fight with
*comment   *gosub_scene combat fight_<name>
*comment and gets back combat_outcome set to "victory", "rescued", or
*comment "fled" once it returns. Nothing in here prints start-of-fight or
*comment end-of-fight prose -- that's deliberate: the kill/retreat and
*comment whatever NPC's reaction to it read as one continuous narrative beat
*comment when the calling quest scene writes both together, instead of a
*comment jarring cut between this file's text and theirs.
*comment
*comment ALLY POOL: combat_ally1-4_* (startup.txt) plus a reusable type library (apply_ally_type/
*comment apply_ally_roster, near fight_library below) -- a scene sets combat_ally_lib_1 (and optionally _2/_3/_4)
*comment to a type id ("archer", "guard") before calling fight_library, same convention as combat_lib_1 for
*comment enemies. Ranged allies attack every turn regardless of distance; melee allies close their own
*comment combat_ally<N>_range (independent of the player's combat_enemy_range) one tier a turn before they can
*comment strike. fight_narrate_ally_attack narrates either direction (ally-vs-enemy, and enemy-vs-ally via
*comment interception below) -- both sides are named purely by epithet, so one label covers both.
*comment
*comment ADDING A FIGHT: use fight_library (near the end of this file). Set
*comment combat_lib_1 (and _2 through _6) to library type ids, set combat_is_nonlethal if it should end in surrender, then *gosub_scene combat fight_library.
*comment Nothing in this file needs editing, and no prose is written per fight. Every fight in the game is built this way;
*comment none is hand-authored. Only a foe no library type fits should be written out by hand, as below.
*comment
*comment Hand-authored alternative: write a new *label fight_<name> block below
*comment that sets the enemy's stats (combat_enemy_*), combat_enemy_epithet,
*comment and SIX flavor triplets, each as _1/_2/_3 (picked at random per hit,
*comment see pick_weapon_flavor / fight_narrate_enemy_turn -- a fight that
*comment only ever prints one fixed sentence per outcome reads as a stuck
*comment record across a multi-round fight):
*comment   combat_flavor_weapon_hit_1/2/3, _miss_1/2/3       (player, melee)
*comment   combat_flavor_weapon_hit_ranged_1/2/3, _miss_ranged_1/2/3
*comment     (player, ranged -- needs its own wording, not melee text with
*comment      ${weapon} swapped in, which reads wrong for a bow/crossbow)
*comment   combat_flavor_enemy_hit_1/2/3, _miss_1/2/3         (enemy, slot 1
*comment     and the default/fallback for slot 2 and beyond)
*comment All three variants for a given line should vary the actual PHYSICAL
*comment ACTION (a thrust vs. a feint-and-cut vs. closing distance under a
*comment guard), not just reshuffle adjectives around the same motion.
*comment Optionally, a distinctly-named multi-enemy fight can also give any slot 2 and beyond its OWN triplets
*comment (combat_flavor_enemy2_hit/miss_1/2/3 through _enemy6_, startup.txt) instead of falling back to the shared
*comment one -- see fight_granary_rats' three rats. (The Dissonant Whispers lines are
*comment no longer per fight: the hub writes them, naming the foe.) Then
*comment *gosub fight_setup and *goto fight_round_hub. Everything else -- every spell/cantrip/
*comment ability option, the enemy's attack resolution, retreat/rescue
*comment handling -- is already shared and needs no duplication.
*comment
*comment Enemies drawn from the type library (apply_enemy_type, see ENEMY TYPE LIBRARY below) need none of the enemy
*comment flavor above: their hit, miss and move-2 prose lives once per type in flavor_type_<name> (eight hit/miss
*comment pairs and three move-2 pairs), and a fight only sets a per-slot variable to replace one line with local color.
*comment
*comment Every cantrip (Fire Bolt, Ray of Frost, Shocking Grasp, Eldritch
*comment Blast, Chill Touch, Vicious Mockery) and buff/utility spell below is
*comment its OWN bespoke *choice option with its own real description and
*comment RAW secondary effect where one maps cleanly onto this engine
*comment (Ray of Frost/Vicious Mockery both set combat_enemy_disadvantage,
*comment matching what the dossier's print_ability_card already promises the
*comment player -- see choicescript_stats.txt). This used to be a single
*comment generic "Channel ${attack_cantrip_name}" option reading four shared
*comment variables (has_attack_cantrip/attack_cantrip_name/_stat/
*comment _dice_sides, populated by a detect_attack_cantrip primitive) --
*comment that was more reusable to write but broke two things: every cantrip
*comment read identically ("the working..."), and the *else-chained
*comment detection only ever exposed the FIRST matching cantrip slot, so a
*comment build with two attack cantrips could never reach the second one in
*comment combat at all. Deliberately avoid an enemy-specific wording in these
*comment options -- they describe the PLAYER's own casting motion, not the
*comment creature (parametrize with ${combat_enemy_epithet} instead) -- so
*comment new ones never need a per-fight override, same as the buff spells.
*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

*comment Stat overrides (see startup.txt): a scene that wants a tougher or weaker fighter than the library
*comment type sets combat_enemy_hp_override, _ac_override and _atk_override (and the combat_enemy2_ / combat_enemy3_
*comment versions) before calling the fight. They are applied here, after every slot has been built and before slot 1 is
*comment snapshotted, so they work on any type and on any fight label. The special move's to-hit bonus moves by the same
*comment amount as the basic attack's, so a +2 captain is +2 on every swing. 999 is "not set"; each is put back to 999 once used.
*temp so_delta 0
*if (not(combat_enemy_hp_override = 999))
  *set combat_enemy_hp combat_enemy_hp_override
  *set combat_enemy_max_hp combat_enemy_hp_override
*if (not(combat_enemy_ac_override = 999))
  *set combat_enemy_ac combat_enemy_ac_override
*if (not(combat_enemy_atk_override = 999))
  *if (combat_enemy_move2_chance > 0)
    *set so_delta combat_enemy_atk_override
    *set so_delta - combat_enemy_atk_bonus
    *set combat_enemy_move2_atk_bonus + so_delta
  *set combat_enemy_atk_bonus combat_enemy_atk_override
*set combat_enemy_hp_override 999
*set combat_enemy_ac_override 999
*set combat_enemy_atk_override 999
*comment Slots 2 and beyond: one generic subroutine (apply_slot_stat_override, below) instead of a hand-copied
*comment block per slot. Widening combat_enemy_slot_cap later needs no change here.
*temp fs_ov_i 2
*label fight_setup_override_loop
*if (fs_ov_i <= combat_enemy_slot_cap)
  *gosub apply_slot_stat_override fs_ov_i
  *set fs_ov_i + 1
  *goto fight_setup_override_loop

*comment needs_advance flag means the very next *goto fight_round_hub (every fight_<name> label already ends with
*comment this, unedited) dispatches through combat_next_turn instead of assuming the player goes first.

*comment Announced here, not inside fight_round_hub, so it's the very first thing shown even when an enemy wins
*comment initiative and attacks before the player ever sees a menu -- fight_round_hub's own banner might not
*comment print until after that first attack.

*label apply_slot_stat_override
*comment One slot's hp/ac/atk override, applied and reset, via name indirection instead of a hand-copied block
*comment per slot (see fight_setup, which calls this for every slot from 2 to combat_enemy_slot_cap; slot 1 keeps
*comment its own block there unchanged, since slot 1's override applies to the plain combat_enemy_* names, not a
*comment numbered slot, until fight_setup snapshots it into combat_enemy1_* afterward).
*params aso_slot
*temp aso_p (("combat_enemy" & aso_slot) & "_")
*temp aso_hp_ov {(aso_p & "hp_override")}
*temp aso_ac_ov {(aso_p & "ac_override")}
*temp aso_atk_ov {(aso_p & "atk_override")}
*temp aso_delta 0
*if (not(aso_hp_ov = 999))
  *set {(aso_p & "hp")} aso_hp_ov
  *set {(aso_p & "max_hp")} aso_hp_ov
*if (not(aso_ac_ov = 999))
  *set {(aso_p & "ac")} aso_ac_ov
*if (not(aso_atk_ov = 999))
  *if ({(aso_p & "move2_chance")} > 0)
    *set aso_delta aso_atk_ov
    *set aso_delta - {(aso_p & "atk_bonus")}
    *set {(aso_p & "move2_atk_bonus")} ({(aso_p & "move2_atk_bonus")} + aso_delta)
  *set {(aso_p & "atk_bonus")} aso_atk_ov
*set {(aso_p & "hp_override")} 999
*set {(aso_p & "ac_override")} 999
*set {(aso_p & "atk_override")} 999
*return

*label apply_ability_score_range
*comment Ability scores, one score for one slot: rolls each of the six for a type-library enemy, feeding
*comment initiative and the derived combat-bonus/AC/HP labels below. The manual per-slot override wins if set
*comment (nonzero, see startup.txt); otherwise this rolls within [aasr_min, aasr_max], the calling type's own
*comment default range. Called six times (once per score) from both branches of every apply_type_X below: slot 1
*comment passes the bare "combat_enemy_" prefix (the same bare names fight_setup later snapshots into
*comment combat_enemy1_*), slot 2-and-beyond passes that type's own apt_p prefix directly. Resets its own override
*comment to 0 once consumed (fight_cleanup also resets every score and override as a backstop, so a hand-authored
*comment fight that never touches ability scores can't inherit a leftover roll from an earlier fight).
*params aasr_prefix aasr_field aasr_min aasr_max
*comment Shares combat_roll_initiative's own whole-fight lock (combat_init_locked/locked_combat_init_page_id,
*comment this file, 2026-09-30) rather than a lock of its own: called many times per fight (once per ability score
*comment per slot) from apply_enemy_type/apply_ally_type, always BEFORE combat_roll_initiative in the same
*comment fight_setup call, on the same first page -- one shared "has this fight's whole setup phase already run
*comment for this page" flag covers both correctly, since a real execution always does both together and a replay
*comment must skip both together. Unlocked, a same-page refresh (fight entry through the first real pause, however
*comment many surprise-round turns deep) rerolled every slot's ability scores from scratch -- a slightly different
*comment STR/DEX cascading into a different combat_enemy_atk_bonus/dmg_bonus (apply_derived_combat_bonuses) even
*comment though nothing about the fight's actual events changed. The one-time manual-override reset below is safe
*comment to skip on a locked replay too -- already consumed down to 0 by the original run, which the snapshot
*comment already reflects.
*if (not(combat_init_locked)) or (not(locked_combat_init_page_id = page_id))
  *temp aasr_ov_name ((aasr_prefix & aasr_field) & "_override")
  *temp aasr_target (aasr_prefix & aasr_field)
  *temp aasr_ov {(aasr_ov_name)}
  *if (aasr_ov > 0)
    *set {(aasr_target)} aasr_ov
  *else
    *temp aasr_roll aasr_min
    *rand aasr_roll aasr_min aasr_max
    *set {(aasr_target)} aasr_roll
  *set {(aasr_ov_name)} 0
*return

*comment ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
*comment DERIVED DEFAULTS: three labels a type can call instead of its own flat *set lines, once
*comment apply_ability_score_range has rolled that slot's six scores. apply_derived_combat_bonuses is wired into
*comment every type in the library; apply_derived_ac/apply_derived_hp are opt-in and not yet called by any of
*comment them, so HP and AC stay each type's own authored flat numbers unless a new type opts in. The formulas
*comment mirror the player's own math rather than inventing new rules: attack/damage bonus off score_to_mod the
*comment same way the player's weapon attacks work, AC the same shape as equipment.txt's recalculate_armor_class
*comment (base_ac + DEX mod), HP the same shape as startup.txt's hp_max (hit dice count * (hit die max + CON
*comment mod)). apply_slot_stat_override's hp/ac/atk overrides still win afterward in every case -- they already
*comment treat whatever value is sitting there as the baseline to overwrite or offset, so they don't care whether
*comment that baseline was a flat number or a computed one.
*comment ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

*label apply_derived_combat_bonuses
*comment A "ranged" weapon_type always attacks off DEX; everything else uses whichever of STR/DEX rolled higher
*comment (the same rule the player's own finesse weapons use), so a DEX-leaning melee type needs no extra flag.
*comment atk_bonus = key ability mod + combat_lib_prof_bonus (flat 2 -- every type in either the enemy or ally
*comment library is early, low-tier content). dmg_bonus = key ability mod alone: RAW, proficiency never adds to
*comment damage, matching how the player's own combat_player_dmg_bonus is already just str_mod/dex_mod with no
*comment separate prof term. Shared by both apply_enemy_type and apply_ally_type (2026-09-28) -- prefix-generic
*comment already, nothing enemy-specific here except the constant it used to hardcode by name, not by parameter.
*comment Shares combat_roll_initiative's own whole-fight lock (combat_init_locked/locked_combat_init_page_id,
*comment 2026-09-30) -- same reasoning as apply_ability_score_range's own lock (its *create comment, startup.txt,
*comment has the full explanation): called only once per slot, always during the same one-time fight_setup phase.
*comment Reads/writes the SHARED sm_mod/sm_mod_str scratch (score_to_mod) alongside every other caller in the
*comment whole game, not just combat -- an unlocked replay recomputing it here on a page that spans a whole
*comment surprise round (no real pause in between) clobbers atk_bonus/dmg_bonus with a fresh mod even though the
*comment ability score it reads is itself already correctly preserved, since RECOMPUTING is not free of side
*comment effects when the computation itself writes through a shared global.
*params adcb_prefix
*if (not(combat_init_locked)) or (not(locked_combat_init_page_id = page_id))
  *temp adcb_str {(adcb_prefix & "strength")}
  *temp adcb_dex {(adcb_prefix & "dexterity")}
  *gosub_scene startup score_to_mod adcb_str
  *temp adcb_str_mod sm_mod
  *gosub_scene startup score_to_mod adcb_dex
  *temp adcb_dex_mod sm_mod
  *temp adcb_key_mod adcb_str_mod
  *if ({(adcb_prefix & "weapon_type")} = "ranged")
    *set adcb_key_mod adcb_dex_mod
  *elseif (adcb_dex_mod > adcb_str_mod)
    *set adcb_key_mod adcb_dex_mod
  *set {(adcb_prefix & "atk_bonus")} (adcb_key_mod + combat_lib_prof_bonus)
  *set {(adcb_prefix & "dmg_bonus")} adcb_key_mod
*return

*label apply_derived_ac
*comment ada_base_ac is the type's own default chassis number (armor/natural toughness), not a final answer --
*comment {prefix}base_ac_override (0 = "use the default") can raise or lower it per fight while still letting the
*comment slot's own rolled DEX apply on top, instead of hand-computing a final AC. No armor-tier DEX cap like the
*comment player's medium/heavy armor: nothing in this library needs that yet, and the final ac_override
*comment (apply_slot_stat_override) still covers a DEX-blind heavy fighter if one ever shows up.
*params ada_prefix ada_base_ac
*temp ada_ov_name ((ada_prefix & "base_ac") & "_override")
*temp ada_ov {(ada_ov_name)}
*if (ada_ov > 0)
  *set ada_base_ac ada_ov
*set {(ada_ov_name)} 0
*temp ada_dex {(ada_prefix & "dexterity")}
*gosub_scene startup score_to_mod ada_dex
*set {(ada_prefix & "ac")} (ada_base_ac + sm_mod)
*return

*label apply_derived_hp
*comment adh_hit_dice_count/adh_hit_die_max are the type's own defaults, not a final answer -- the per-slot
*comment overrides (0 = "use the default") can make one instance tougher or squishier while still letting its own
*comment rolled CON apply on top. Same formula startup.txt's hp_max uses. Clamped to a floor of 1 so a
*comment single-hit-die type with a very low CON can never come out dead on arrival.
*params adh_prefix adh_hit_dice_count adh_hit_die_max
*temp adh_hdc_ov_name ((adh_prefix & "hit_dice_count") & "_override")
*temp adh_hdc_ov {(adh_hdc_ov_name)}
*if (adh_hdc_ov > 0)
  *set adh_hit_dice_count adh_hdc_ov
*set {(adh_hdc_ov_name)} 0
*temp adh_hdm_ov_name ((adh_prefix & "hit_die_max") & "_override")
*temp adh_hdm_ov {(adh_hdm_ov_name)}
*if (adh_hdm_ov > 0)
  *set adh_hit_die_max adh_hdm_ov
*set {(adh_hdm_ov_name)} 0
*temp adh_con {(adh_prefix & "constitution")}
*gosub_scene startup score_to_mod adh_con
*temp adh_hp (adh_hit_dice_count * (adh_hit_die_max + sm_mod))
*if (adh_hp < 1)
  *set adh_hp 1
*set {(adh_prefix & "hp")} adh_hp
*set {(adh_prefix & "max_hp")} adh_hp
*return

*label cri_is_better_than_best
*comment Compares initiative candidate cri_ibt_a against candidate cri_ibt_b (params: 1-indexed positions into
*comment the combat_init_* scratch arrays, startup.txt) for combat_roll_initiative's selection sort below. Output
*comment cri_ibt_result: true if A should be placed ahead of B. Tie-break order, all deterministic so it's
*comment testable: higher initiative roll, then higher raw DEX score (5e's own tiebreak), then player over ally
*comment over enemy, then ascending slot number.
*params cri_ibt_a cri_ibt_b
*temp cri_ibt_a_score {("combat_init_score_" & cri_ibt_a)}
*temp cri_ibt_b_score {("combat_init_score_" & cri_ibt_b)}
*set cri_ibt_result false
*if (cri_ibt_a_score > cri_ibt_b_score)
  *set cri_ibt_result true
*elseif (cri_ibt_a_score = cri_ibt_b_score)
  *temp cri_ibt_a_dex {("combat_init_dex_" & cri_ibt_a)}
  *temp cri_ibt_b_dex {("combat_init_dex_" & cri_ibt_b)}
  *if (cri_ibt_a_dex > cri_ibt_b_dex)
    *set cri_ibt_result true
  *elseif (cri_ibt_a_dex = cri_ibt_b_dex)
    *temp cri_ibt_a_kind {("combat_init_kind_" & cri_ibt_a)}
    *temp cri_ibt_b_kind {("combat_init_kind_" & cri_ibt_b)}
    *temp cri_ibt_a_rank 1
    *temp cri_ibt_b_rank 1
    *if (cri_ibt_a_kind = "player")
      *set cri_ibt_a_rank 3
    *elseif (cri_ibt_a_kind = "ally")
      *set cri_ibt_a_rank 2
    *if (cri_ibt_b_kind = "player")
      *set cri_ibt_b_rank 3
    *elseif (cri_ibt_b_kind = "ally")
      *set cri_ibt_b_rank 2
    *if (cri_ibt_a_rank > cri_ibt_b_rank)
      *set cri_ibt_result true
    *elseif (cri_ibt_a_rank = cri_ibt_b_rank)
      *temp cri_ibt_a_slot {("combat_init_slot_" & cri_ibt_a)}
      *temp cri_ibt_b_slot {("combat_init_slot_" & cri_ibt_b)}
      *if (cri_ibt_a_slot < cri_ibt_b_slot)
        *set cri_ibt_result true
*return

*label combat_roll_initiative
*comment Rolled ONCE per fight, from fight_setup after every slot is built -- not every round, 5e's own rule.
*comment Candidates are the player plus every POPULATED ally slot (1..combat_ally_count) and enemy slot
*comment (1..combat_enemy_count); an unpopulated slot beyond that never gets an entry, so a 1-enemy fight builds
*comment a 2-long order (player + that one enemy), not an 11-long one padded with empty slots. Builds
*comment combat_turn_kind_1..N/_slot_1..N (startup.txt), consumed by combat_next_turn below.
*comment Whole-body replay lock (2026-09-30, real bug caught live via refresh_fuzz_test.js on a fresh random seed):
*comment completely unguarded until now -- fight_setup calls this once, up front, then falls straight through
*comment combat_begin_round/announce_turn_order into combat_next_turn's own dispatch of everyone who acts before
*comment the player, all on the SAME page (no *choice pause yet). A refresh at the first REAL pause (however many
*comment turns deep that surprise round went) replays from the fight's own entry point and reaches this call
*comment again, rerolling every combatant's initiative from scratch -- a completely different turn order, which
*comment cascades into different attackers, different targets, different damage, the works. Structurally this
*comment already only runs once per fight in normal forward play (nothing else ever calls it again mid-fight), so
*comment the standard whole-body self-lock (combat_weapon_attack_locked's shape, this file) is exactly right: no
*comment per-slot index needed, and once a fight moves past its first page this lock is simply never consulted
*comment again, harmlessly. announce_turn_order (below) stays unlocked -- it only reads combat_turn_kind_N/
*comment _slot_N back and prints them, so it reprints correctly off whatever this lock preserved.
*if (not(combat_init_locked)) or (not(locked_combat_init_page_id = page_id))
  *temp cri_n 0
  *set cri_n + 1
  *set combat_init_kind_1 "player"
  *set combat_init_slot_1 0
  *set combat_init_dex_1 dexterity
  *temp cri_roll 1
  *rand cri_roll 1 20
  *gosub_scene startup score_to_mod dexterity
  *set combat_init_score_1 (cri_roll + sm_mod)
  *temp cri_i 1
  *label cri_ally_loop
  *if (cri_i <= combat_ally_count)
    *set cri_n + 1
    *set {("combat_init_kind_" & cri_n)} "ally"
    *set {("combat_init_slot_" & cri_n)} cri_i
    *temp cri_a_dex {(("combat_ally" & cri_i) & "_dexterity")}
    *set {("combat_init_dex_" & cri_n)} cri_a_dex
    *temp cri_a_roll 1
    *rand cri_a_roll 1 20
    *gosub_scene startup score_to_mod cri_a_dex
    *set {("combat_init_score_" & cri_n)} (cri_a_roll + sm_mod)
    *set cri_i + 1
    *goto cri_ally_loop
  *temp cri_j 1
  *label cri_enemy_loop
  *if (cri_j <= combat_enemy_count)
    *set cri_n + 1
    *set {("combat_init_kind_" & cri_n)} "enemy"
    *set {("combat_init_slot_" & cri_n)} cri_j
    *temp cri_e_dex {(("combat_enemy" & cri_j) & "_dexterity")}
    *set {("combat_init_dex_" & cri_n)} cri_e_dex
    *temp cri_e_roll 1
    *rand cri_e_roll 1 20
    *gosub_scene startup score_to_mod cri_e_dex
    *set {("combat_init_score_" & cri_n)} (cri_e_roll + sm_mod)
    *set cri_j + 1
    *goto cri_enemy_loop
  *set combat_init_count cri_n
  *comment Stale "used" flags from an earlier fight's sort must be cleared before this one's -- these are
  *comment persistent *create'd storage, not fresh *temps, so they don't reset themselves between calls.
  *temp cri_reset_i 1
  *label cri_reset_loop
  *if (cri_reset_i <= 11)
    *set {("combat_init_used_" & cri_reset_i)} false
    *set cri_reset_i + 1
    *goto cri_reset_loop
  *comment Selection sort, descending: repeatedly scan every not-yet-placed candidate for the best remaining one
  *comment (cri_is_better_than_best above), place it, mark it used. Simpler and safer than an insertion sort's
  *comment shifting step, and with at most 11 candidates the O(n^2) cost is nothing.
  *temp cri_placed 0
  *label cri_sort_loop
  *if (cri_placed < combat_init_count)
    *temp cri_best 0
    *temp cri_k 1
    *label cri_scan_loop
    *if (cri_k <= combat_init_count)
      *if (not({("combat_init_used_" & cri_k)}))
        *if (cri_best = 0)
          *set cri_best cri_k
        *else
          *gosub cri_is_better_than_best cri_k cri_best
          *if (cri_ibt_result)
            *set cri_best cri_k
      *set cri_k + 1
      *goto cri_scan_loop
    *set cri_placed + 1
    *set {("combat_turn_kind_" & cri_placed)} {("combat_init_kind_" & cri_best)}
    *set {("combat_turn_slot_" & cri_placed)} {("combat_init_slot_" & cri_best)}
    *set {("combat_init_used_" & cri_best)} true
    *goto cri_sort_loop
  *set combat_turn_count combat_init_count
  *comment Reset every position's first-pass flag true for this fight (startup.txt) -- combat_next_turn clears one
  *comment for good the instant that position dispatches, so a stale "already went" from a previous fight can never
  *comment suppress this fight's surprise round.
  *temp cri_fp_i 1
  *label cri_fp_reset_loop
  *if (cri_fp_i <= 11)
    *set {("combat_turn_first_pass_" & cri_fp_i)} true
    *set cri_fp_i + 1
    *goto cri_fp_reset_loop
  *set combat_init_locked true
  *set locked_combat_init_page_id page_id
*return

*label announce_turn_order
*comment One line, once, right after combat_roll_initiative builds the order -- the only player-visible
*comment confirmation the turn queue exists at all. A real name per position (bare "You" for the player, matching
*comment how the banner already says "You:" not the character's own name). Printed once because the order never
*comment changes for the rest of the fight -- a defeated combatant's turn is simply skipped when reached.
*temp ato_line ""
*temp ato_i 1
*label announce_turn_order_loop
*if (ato_i <= combat_turn_count)
  *temp ato_kind {("combat_turn_kind_" & ato_i)}
  *temp ato_slot {("combat_turn_slot_" & ato_i)}
  *temp ato_name "You"
  *if (ato_kind = "ally")
    *set ato_name {(("combat_ally" & ato_slot) & "_name")}
  *if (ato_kind = "enemy")
    *set ato_name {(("combat_enemy" & ato_slot) & "_name")}
  *if (ato_line = "")
    *set ato_line ato_name
  *else
    *set ato_line ((ato_line & ", ") & ato_name)
  *set ato_i + 1
  *goto announce_turn_order_loop
*comment A blank source line, not *line_break: this is always the very first thing combat.txt prints on a fresh
*comment entry, so the engine's paragraph buffer is always empty here -- *line_break would take its "no open
*comment paragraph" fallback (a bare <br> outside any <p>) every time, which stacks with the calling scene's own
*comment closing <p> margin into a bigger gap than a normal paragraph break. A blank line just closes whatever's
*comment open (a no-op when there's nothing to close), so the calling scene's last line and this banner get
*comment ordinary, single-size paragraph spacing either way.

[b][⚡ Turn Order: ${ato_line}][/b]
*line_break
*return

*comment Ally pool: reset per-slot range, target and library id, the same leftover-state guard as the enemy
*comment range/lib resets in the loop just above. combat_ally_count 0 (already set) stops anything from reading
*comment a stale slot's other fields -- apply_ally_type always fully rewrites name/hp/ac/etc. for any slot a

  *comment just above: a guard reusing a slot an archer used last fight must not inherit her retreat fields.
  *set {(("combat_ally" & fc_a_i) & "_can_retreat")} false
  *set {(("combat_ally" & fc_a_i) & "_flee_hp_pct")} 0
  *set fc_a_i + 1

*comment ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
*comment MULTI-ENEMY TARGETING (up to 3 simultaneous enemy slots)
*comment Slot 1/2/3 (combat_enemy1_*/2_*/3_*, startup.txt) are each enemy's
*comment real, permanent stats. The canonical combat_enemy_* names (no
*comment number) are a scratch mirror of whichever slot is "active" --
*comment that's what every shared primitive in startup.txt (resolve_player_
*comment hit, resolve_enemy_attack, etc.) actually reads and writes, so they
*comment never needed to learn about multiple enemies at all. These three
*comment subroutines are the entire seam: sync the active slot in before a
*comment primitive touches it, sync it back out after, and pick which slot
*comment is active (skipping defeated ones, slot order 1/2/3).

*label fight_sync_target_out
*set {(("combat_enemy" & combat_active_target) & "_hp")} combat_enemy_hp
