*comment Range tiers: 0 Engaged, 1 Close, 2 Mid-Range, 3 Long Range. A melee-only combatant closes exactly one
*comment tier per Push Forward (player) or per turn (an enemy, combat_next_turn) -- never straight to Engaged --
*comment so a fight that opens someone at Mid-Range or Long Range costs multiple turns to reach, not one. Ranged
*comment attacks otherwise work at every tier alike, which is why nothing here ever needs a ranged combatant to
*comment move. **The one exception: a ranged attack against a target at range 0 is made at disadvantage** ("Ranged
*comment Attacks in Close Combat"). It applies to a weapon, a spell, or any other means, to the ENEMY
*comment (resolve_enemy_attack, combat_dice.txt) and to the PLAYER (resolve_weapon_attack_choice below and the
*comment cantrip options, via spell_range_of) alike, and it is unconditional -- one foe or eight is the same
*comment single disadvantage. Melee attacks are never penalised for being in melee, so closing on an archer is no
*comment longer pure punishment for the player, and a ranged combatant finally has a reason to want distance.
*comment See resolve_enemy_attack's header for the full reasoning.
*label combat_range_name
*comment *gosub combat_range_name <range number>, then read combat_range_display_name (startup.txt *create) --
*comment a durable output var instead of a caller-pre-declared *temp, since this label sits above every caller
*comment in file order. Anything past 3 still reads as Long Range, rather than falling through blank, in case a
*comment future fight sets a range higher than today's four tiers.
*params crn_n
*set combat_range_display_name "Engaged"
*if (crn_n = 1)
  *set combat_range_display_name "Close"
*if (crn_n = 2)
  *set combat_range_display_name "Mid-Range"
*if (crn_n >= 3)
  *set combat_range_display_name "Long Range"
*return

*label spell_range_of
*comment ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
*comment SPELL REACH TAG LOOKUP
*comment Resolves a spell's reach from check_skill, the variable every attack option already sets to the
*comment spell's display name before it rolls. Generated from tools/spell_catalog.json -- edit the catalog,
*comment never this block (node tools/gen_spells.js regenerates it).
*comment
*comment WHY resolve from check_skill: the tag answers two DIFFERENT questions, and combat.txt asks them
*comment separately. Collapsing them is a real bug -- a touch spell at Mid-Range must be UNAVAILABLE, not
*comment offered at a disadvantage.
*comment   can the caster reach the target at all?  -> spell_reach_result gates whether the option is offered
*comment   is the shot spoiled by a hostile in reach? -> spell_penalty_result sets disadvantage
*comment
*comment TAGS (three, so the first spell needing close or touch costs nothing to add later):
*comment   ranged  any tier; DISADVANTAGE at Engaged (the close-combat ranged penalty, same for a weapon, a
*comment           thrown weapon, or a spell). This is the ONLY tag that can take a penalty.
*comment   close   Close (1) or Mid-Range (2) only -- never Engaged. A melee enemy closing on the caster takes
*comment           the spell away entirely, which is the whole point. No penalty of its own: the target is
*comment           never adjacent. Reserved; no spell uses it yet.
*comment   touch   Engaged (0) only. No penalty -- a touch spell IS a melee attack, and melee attacks are never
*comment           penalised for being in melee.
*comment
*comment MELEE ATTACKS ARE NEVER PENALISED. The rule is one-directional: a ranged attack made against a
*comment creature in reach is spoiled; a melee attack made by someone in reach is not. Unarmed strikes are
*comment melee (equipment.txt's "unarmed" weapon_type), so they are never penalised either.
*comment
*comment Reads combat_enemy_range -- the ACTIVE enemy's distance to the player, kept current by
*comment fight_sync_target_in. KNOWN LIMIT: that is one distance, relative to the PLAYER. A spell that hit
*comment every enemy near a point (a cone, a burst) would need a real per-enemy distance; no spell does that
*comment yet, so nothing needs it, and a later cone spell must not be quietly given this same shortcut.
*comment Defaults are the common case (ranged, in range, no penalty) so an unlisted name -- a leveled spell
*comment not yet catalogued -- behaves like a ranged spell rather than silently disabling the option.
*comment GEN:BEGIN spell_range_lookup -- generated by tools/gen_spells.js from tools/spell_catalog.json; edit the catalog, not this block
*temp spell_range_tag "ranged"
*temp spell_in_range true
*temp spell_disadv false
*comment Read the TARGET's live distance, not the shared combat_enemy_range scratch. fight_round_hub calls
*comment fight_pick_target (setting combat_active_target) but not fight_sync_target_in, so that scratch is
*comment STALE at menu-build time -- a fight opened at Long Range still read 0 on its first page. The option
*comment bodies below call fight_pick_target first, so combat_active_target is the slot this cast will hit;
*comment combat_target_range is that slot's own range, computed fresh in the hub (see the block above the
*comment *choice). The scratch is only the fallback for a call made before the hub has run.
*temp spell_range_at combat_enemy_range
*if (combat_target_range >= 0)
  *set spell_range_at combat_target_range
*if (check_skill = "Fire Bolt")
  *set spell_range_tag "ranged"
*if (check_skill = "Ray of Frost")
  *set spell_range_tag "ranged"
*if (check_skill = "Eldritch Blast")
  *set spell_range_tag "ranged"
*if (check_skill = "Chill Touch")
  *set spell_range_tag "ranged"
*if (check_skill = "Vicious Mockery")
  *set spell_range_tag "ranged"
*if (check_skill = "Shocking Grasp")
  *set spell_range_tag "touch"
*if (spell_range_tag = "close")
  *if (spell_range_at <= 0)
    *set spell_in_range false
*if (spell_range_tag = "touch")
  *if (spell_range_at > 0)
    *set spell_in_range false
*if (spell_range_tag = "ranged")
  *if (spell_range_at <= 0)
    *set spell_disadv true
*set spell_reach_result spell_in_range
*set spell_penalty_result spell_disadv
*comment GEN:END spell_range_lookup
*return

*label combat_mm_recount_darts
*comment Sums every enemy slot's combat_mm_darts_N into the durable combat_mm_darts_total (startup.txt
*comment *create), read by fight_target_magic_missile's Cast option. The sidebar (web/mygame/index.html) writes
*comment the per-slot fields directly via window.stats and never touches this total itself -- it's a
*comment ChoiceScript-side convenience so callers don't each write their own bounded loop.
*set combat_mm_darts_total 0
*temp cmrd_i 1
*label combat_mm_recount_darts_loop
*if (cmrd_i <= combat_enemy_slot_cap)
  *set combat_mm_darts_total + {(("combat_mm_darts_" & cmrd_i))}
  *set cmrd_i + 1
  *goto combat_mm_recount_darts_loop
*return

*label find_flanking_ally
*comment Sets combat_flank_ally to a living ally at range 0, or 0 for none. Used by Sneak Attack's ally trigger
*comment (resolve_player_weapon_hit, combat_dice.txt). Lowest slot wins; it only needs to know whether one exists.
*set combat_flank_ally 0
*temp ffa_i 1
*label find_flanking_ally_loop
*if (ffa_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
  *temp ffa_hp {(("combat_ally" & ffa_i) & "_hp")}
  *temp ffa_range {(("combat_ally" & ffa_i) & "_range")}
  *if ((combat_flank_ally = 0) and ((ffa_hp > 0) and (ffa_range <= 0)))
    *set combat_flank_ally ffa_i
  *set ffa_i + 1
  *goto find_flanking_ally_loop
*return

*label combat_hidden_attack_roll
*comment Called immediately before every player attack roll (the weapon roll and each spell attack's roll). A hidden player
*comment attacks with advantage, and making the attack gives the position away whether it hits or misses, so the flag is
*comment spent here, before the roll, not after it. That is the only place advantage is applied, so a new attack option
*comment adds this one line and nothing else. Advantage still cancels against any disadvantage already set (reach, etc.),
*comment same as everywhere else. Sneak Attack reads roll_had_advantage, so it fires on its own from here.
*if (combat_player_hidden)
  *set combat_player_hidden false
  *set advantage true
  [b][👤 You strike from hiding, and your cover breaks.][/b]
  *line_break
*return

*label combat_check_hide
*comment Hide needs open ground between you and every living enemy (2026-10-01): no enemy at Close (1) or Engaged (0).
*comment Reads each slot's own range, not the shared combat_enemy_range scratch, which only follows one slot.
*set combat_hide_blocked false
*temp cch_i 1
*label combat_check_hide_loop
*if (cch_i <= combat_enemy_count)
  *temp cch_hp {(("combat_enemy" & cch_i) & "_hp")}
  *temp cch_range {(("combat_enemy" & cch_i) & "_range")}
  *if ((cch_hp > 0) and (cch_range <= 1))
    *set combat_hide_blocked true
  *set cch_i + 1
  *goto combat_check_hide_loop
*return

*label combat_inspire_pick
*comment Bardic Inspiration: the first living ally, or 0 when the Bard stands alone (then the die goes to the Bard).
*set combat_inspire_ally_pick 0
*temp cip_i 1
*label combat_inspire_pick_loop
*if (cip_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
  *temp cip_hp {(("combat_ally" & cip_i) & "_hp")}
  *if ((cip_hp > 0) and (combat_inspire_ally_pick = 0))
    *set combat_inspire_ally_pick cip_i
  *set cip_i + 1
  *goto combat_inspire_pick_loop
*return

*label combat_dual_shot_check
*comment Dual Shot (2026-10-02): sets combat_dual_pair_ok when two living enemies stand at the same range tier. The engine has no distance between two
*comment particular creatures, so "within 10 feet of the first target" is approximated as "the same range tier", the same shared-bucket idea the interception
*comment and flanking checks use.
*set combat_dual_pair_ok false
*temp cdc_i 1
*label combat_dual_shot_check_i
*if (cdc_i <= combat_enemy_count)
  *temp cdc_ihp {(("combat_enemy" & cdc_i) & "_hp")}
  *temp cdc_irange {(("combat_enemy" & cdc_i) & "_range")}
  *if (cdc_ihp > 0)
    *temp cdc_j (cdc_i + 1)
    *label combat_dual_shot_check_j
    *if (cdc_j <= combat_enemy_count)
      *temp cdc_jhp {(("combat_enemy" & cdc_j) & "_hp")}
      *temp cdc_jrange {(("combat_enemy" & cdc_j) & "_range")}
      *if ((cdc_jhp > 0) and (cdc_jrange = cdc_irange))
        *set combat_dual_pair_ok true
      *set cdc_j + 1
      *goto combat_dual_shot_check_j
  *set cdc_i + 1
  *goto combat_dual_shot_check_i
*return

*label combat_dual_shot_validate
*comment Reads what the player assigned in the sidebar (combat_mm_darts_N, max 2). Valid means exactly two different living enemies with one shot each, at the same
*comment range tier. Sets combat_dual_a/_b to those slots, or combat_dual_why to the reason it is not.
*set combat_dual_valid false
*set combat_dual_a 0
*set combat_dual_b 0
*set combat_dual_why ""
*gosub combat_mm_recount_darts
*temp cdv_i 1
*temp cdv_slots 0
*temp cdv_over false
*label combat_dual_shot_validate_loop
*if (cdv_i <= combat_enemy_slot_cap)
  *temp cdv_n {(("combat_mm_darts_" & cdv_i))}
  *if (cdv_n > 0)
    *set cdv_slots + 1
    *if (cdv_n > 1)
      *set cdv_over true
    *if (combat_dual_a = 0)
      *set combat_dual_a cdv_i
    *else
      *set combat_dual_b cdv_i
  *set cdv_i + 1
  *goto combat_dual_shot_validate_loop
*if (combat_mm_darts_total < 2)
  *set combat_dual_why "Place both shots first: click an enemy in the sidebar for each."
*elseif (cdv_over)
  *set combat_dual_why "The two shots must go to two different creatures."
*else
  *temp cdv_arange {(("combat_enemy" & combat_dual_a) & "_range")}
  *temp cdv_brange {(("combat_enemy" & combat_dual_b) & "_range")}
  *if (not(cdv_arange = cdv_brange))
    *set combat_dual_why "The two targets must be at the same range, close enough together to bracket with one draw."
  *else
    *set combat_dual_valid true
*return

*label combat_hm_state
*comment Hunter's Mark state, refreshed every time the hub opens (see startup.txt's *create comment). Live: the mark is unexpired and its
*comment creature still stands. Orphaned: unexpired, but that creature has dropped, so the mark can be moved with a free bonus action.
*set combat_hm_live false
*set combat_hm_orphaned false
*set combat_hm_name ""
*if (character_class = "ranger")
  *if ((favored_foe_target_slot > 0) and (favored_foe_expires_round >= combat_round))
    *temp chs_hp {(("combat_enemy" & favored_foe_target_slot) & "_hp")}
    *if (chs_hp > 0)
      *set combat_hm_live true
      *set combat_hm_name {(("combat_enemy" & favored_foe_target_slot) & "_name")}
    *else
      *set combat_hm_orphaned true
*return

*label fight_pick_target
*comment Kill priority (2026-09-28): the lowest-HP living slot, not the lowest slot number -- finishes off
*comment whichever enemy is already wounded instead of always working strictly front-to-back. Shared by the
*comment player's own HUD-fallback targeting AND every ally's turn (combat_next_turn), so both get smarter from
*comment one change, not duplicated per caller -- the first piece of a wider smarter-AI pass (see
*comment GAMEPLAY_MECHANICS_RULES.md). A HUD-clicked target (combat_player_chosen_target, sticky until it dies --
*comment see startup.txt) still wins over it whenever one is set and still alive, checked first, below.
*set combat_active_target 0
*if (combat_player_chosen_target > 0)
  *temp fpt_chosen_hp {(("combat_enemy" & combat_player_chosen_target) & "_hp")}
  *if ((combat_player_chosen_target <= combat_enemy_count) and (fpt_chosen_hp > 0))
    *set combat_active_target combat_player_chosen_target
  *else
    *comment The chosen target just died (or the fight shrank under it) -- clear it so the HUD stops
    *comment highlighting a dead slot, and fall through to the ordinary auto-pick below.
    *set combat_player_chosen_target 0
*if (combat_active_target = 0)
  *comment Scans every slot rather than stopping at the first living one (the old front-to-back pick's
  *comment shortcut) -- the lowest-HP slot can be anywhere in the order, so every living slot needs comparing.
  *temp fpt_i 1
  *temp fpt_best_hp 0
  *label fight_pick_target_loop
  *if (fpt_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp fpt_hp {(("combat_enemy" & fpt_i) & "_hp")}
    *if (fpt_hp > 0)
      *if ((combat_active_target = 0) or (fpt_hp < fpt_best_hp))
        *set combat_active_target fpt_i
        *set fpt_best_hp fpt_hp
    *set fpt_i + 1
    *goto fight_pick_target_loop
*if (combat_active_target > 0)
  *gosub fight_sync_target_in
*return

*label fight_pick_nearest_target
*comment Movement targeting (2026-09-28): the NEAREST living enemy that's actually eligible for this direction,
*comment not kill-priority -- Push Forward/Fall Back are about closing or opening a specific gap, so "who's
*comment already closest" is the sensible default, not "who's most wounded" (fight_pick_target above, used for
*comment attacks and ally targeting instead). "push" wants the nearest enemy with range > 0 (nothing to gain
*comment closing on one already Engaged); "fallback" wants the nearest with range < 3 (nothing to gain opening on
*comment one already at Long Range) -- an enemy Engaged with you is a valid Fall Back candidate but never a valid
*comment Push Forward one, so different enemies can legitimately win depending on direction. A HUD-clicked target
*comment (combat_player_chosen_target, sticky until it dies) still wins over the auto-pick whenever it's alive
*comment AND eligible for this direction; alive-but-ineligible falls through to the ordinary scan below rather
*comment than silently doing nothing (the click stays sticky for its original purpose, attacks, either way).
*params fpnt_direction
*set combat_active_target 0
*if (combat_player_chosen_target > 0)
  *temp fpnt_chosen_hp {(("combat_enemy" & combat_player_chosen_target) & "_hp")}
  *if ((combat_player_chosen_target > combat_enemy_count) or (fpnt_chosen_hp <= 0))
    *comment Dead, or the fight shrank under the click -- clear it for the same reason fight_pick_target
    *comment does, so the HUD stops highlighting a slot that no longer holds anything.
    *set combat_player_chosen_target 0
  *else
    *comment Alive and in range. Eligibility is checked here but must NEVER clear the click (2026-09-28).
    *comment **This branch used to be written as an inner *if for eligibility with the *else bound to THAT
    *comment if**, so being ineligible for one movement direction silently wiped combat_player_chosen_target
    *comment -- meaning "click the swordsman, then Push forward" forgot who you were attacking, and your next
    *comment swing reverted to kill-priority. The header comment above already promised the opposite ("the
    *comment click stays sticky for its original purpose, attacks, either way"); the code just didn't do it.
    *comment Inverting the test puts the *else back on the liveness check, which is the only thing that
    *comment should ever clear a click. It became materially more reachable once the movement buttons started
    *comment advertising a from/to tier, which invites exactly that click-then-move sequence.
    *temp fpnt_chosen_range {(("combat_enemy" & combat_player_chosen_target) & "_range")}
    *if (((fpnt_direction = "push") and (fpnt_chosen_range > 0)) or ((fpnt_direction = "fallback") and (fpnt_chosen_range < 3)))
      *set combat_active_target combat_player_chosen_target
*if (combat_active_target = 0)
  *temp fpnt_i 1
  *temp fpnt_best_range 0
  *label fight_pick_nearest_target_loop
  *if (fpnt_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp fpnt_hp {(("combat_enemy" & fpnt_i) & "_hp")}
    *if (fpnt_hp > 0)
      *temp fpnt_range {(("combat_enemy" & fpnt_i) & "_range")}
      *temp fpnt_ok false
      *if ((fpnt_direction = "push") and (fpnt_range > 0))
        *set fpnt_ok true
      *if ((fpnt_direction = "fallback") and (fpnt_range < 3))
        *set fpnt_ok true
      *if (fpnt_ok)
        *if ((combat_active_target = 0) or (fpnt_range < fpnt_best_range))
          *set combat_active_target fpnt_i
          *set fpnt_best_range fpnt_range
    *set fpnt_i + 1
    *goto fight_pick_nearest_target_loop
*if (combat_active_target > 0)
  *gosub fight_sync_target_in
*return

*label combat_shift_all_ranges
*comment Applies one range tier to EVERY living enemy at once, not just the nearest eligible one (2026-09-28).
*comment A single step back physically moves you away from the whole fight, so the old single-slot write was
*comment only correct for the one enemy the move was narrated against: with two enemies Engaged, falling back
*comment left one reading Close and the other Engaged. That was invisible while nothing rendered range, and read
*comment as the engine disagreeing with itself the moment the sidebar began printing a range tag per row. It also
*comment brings the effect in line with the availability gates, which have always been computed across every
*comment living enemy (an enemy can't be simultaneously at range 0 and range 3, so with two or more living
*comment enemies one of Push Forward/Fall Back is always legal for somebody).
*comment Calling convention: *gosub combat_shift_all_ranges "push" / "fallback" -- a direction string rather than
*comment a numeric delta, matching fight_pick_nearest_target above, so no caller has to hand this a negative
*comment literal. Clamps every slot to the 0-3 tier scale and skips dead ones.
*comment Writes the slots directly by indirection and deliberately does NOT re-sync the active-target scratch:
*comment each caller has just picked its narration target with fight_pick_nearest_target, so it re-syncs once
*comment with fight_sync_target_in after this returns, to pick up the nearest enemy's new value.
*params csar_direction
*comment Self-locked (2026-09-30, real bug caught live via refresh_fuzz_test.js): a bare per-slot mutation with
*comment no guard at all -- Push Forward/Fall Back/Break-off-and-run (its three call sites) are mutually
*comment exclusive *choice options, so a page-id lock alone is sufficient, no per-call-site id needed the way
*comment a subroutine genuinely called twice on one page would (see GAMEPLAY_MECHANICS_RULES.md). Without this, a
*comment refresh replaying the current page from its start re-shifted every living enemy's range a second time --
*comment found via combat_enemy_range/combat_range_display_name diverging on refresh, symptomatic of the same
*comment root cause as every other bare *set var +N in this codebase's history.
*if (not(combat_range_shift_locked)) or (not(locked_combat_range_shift_page_id = page_id))
  *temp csar_delta 1
  *if (csar_direction = "push")
    *set csar_delta -1
  *temp csar_i 1
  *label combat_shift_all_ranges_loop
  *if (csar_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp csar_hp {(("combat_enemy" & csar_i) & "_hp")}
    *if (csar_hp > 0)
      *temp csar_from {(("combat_enemy" & csar_i) & "_range")}
      *temp csar_to (csar_from + csar_delta)
      *if (csar_to < 0)
        *set csar_to 0
      *if (csar_to > 3)
        *set csar_to 3
      *set {(("combat_enemy" & csar_i) & "_range")} csar_to
    *set csar_i + 1
    *goto combat_shift_all_ranges_loop
  *set combat_range_shift_locked true
  *set locked_combat_range_shift_page_id page_id
*return

*label find_opportunity_attacker
*comment Opportunity attacks (2026-09-28). Sets combat_oa_candidate to the enemy that gets to swing as the
*comment player leaves, or 0 for none. Must be called BEFORE the move that opens the tier, while every enemy
*comment is still at its pre-move range -- checked afterwards, range 1 cannot tell "just left melee" apart
*comment from "was never in melee", and both look identical.
*comment
*comment THE CAP IS THE WHOLE POINT. combat_shift_all_ranges moves EVERY enemy at once, so a Fall Back from three
*comment engaged enemies opens a tier on all three and RAW would fire three separate opportunity attacks. The
*comment engine has no player reaction budget at all (see the turn-budget comment: "REACTION: not a budget
*comment yet"), so that is roughly eighteen damage for one retreat step and an unwinnable spiral. **One OA per
*comment move, maximum**, which is also the most defensible reading -- you only have to get past one of them.
*comment This is the same decision as the per-round cap on combat_enemyN_oa_used, and the two together mean a
*comment single enemy can hurt you at most once per round no matter how much you move around it.
*comment
*comment CANDIDATE RULES, all four required: living; melee weapon_type (a 5e opportunity attack is a MELEE
*comment attack, and a ranged enemy already at melee suffers the close-range disadvantage penalty instead, so
*comment giving it a parting shot too would double-punish the same approach); range 0, i.e. actually in reach of
*comment the player, which is the entire trigger; and oa_used false, its reaction for this round still spare.
*comment Lowest HP wins, mirroring find_intercepting_ally's tiebreak for consistency -- finish off whoever is
*comment nearly down rather than whoever stands nearest, and ties fall to the lowest slot via a strict <.
*comment
*comment Suppressed by combat_player_disengaged at the CALL SITE, not here, so the two departure options (Fall
*comment Back and Break off) cannot drift apart on when the suppression applies.
*set combat_oa_candidate 0
*temp foa_best_hp 0
*temp foa_i 1
*label find_opportunity_attacker_loop
*if (foa_i <= combat_enemy_count)
  *temp foa_hp {(("combat_enemy" & foa_i) & "_hp")}
  *temp foa_weapon {(("combat_enemy" & foa_i) & "_weapon_type")}
  *temp foa_range {(("combat_enemy" & foa_i) & "_range")}
  *temp foa_oa {(("combat_enemy" & foa_i) & "_oa_used")}
  *if ((((foa_hp > 0) and (foa_weapon = "melee")) and (foa_range <= 0)) and (foa_oa = false))
    *if ((combat_oa_candidate = 0) or (foa_hp < foa_best_hp))
      *set combat_oa_candidate foa_i
      *set foa_best_hp foa_hp
  *set foa_i + 1
  *goto find_opportunity_attacker_loop
*return

*label resolve_opportunity_attack
*comment Resolves the swing find_opportunity_attacker picked, called AFTER the tier has actually opened and only
*comment when combat_oa_candidate is non-zero.
*comment
*comment **Deliberately reuses resolve_enemy_attack and fight_narrate_enemy_turn whole, with no parallel path.**
*comment That is what makes an opportunity attack behave like every other hit the player takes, for free: Shield
*comment can still break through it, Armor of Agathys still bites back, Blade Ward still halves it, resistance,
*comment vulnerability, temp HP, conditions and stun all still apply, and the death and rescue checks still run.
*comment Writing a "simpler" OA resolver would have quietly forked all of that. fight_narrate_enemy_turn is
*comment equally safe to reuse: it narrates "this enemy attacks you" and nothing about closing distance, which
*comment lives in combat_next_turn's own branch instead.
*comment
*comment Deliberately does NOT call fight_pick_enemy_move, so no move-2 proc -- a reaction is a basic attack.
*comment
*comment Clears combat_oa_candidate and spends the enemy's oa_used BEFORE rolling, so a second departure later on
*comment the same page can never reach the roll at all. That also keeps it clear of the replay lock, which would
*comment otherwise find combat_enemy_atk_locked_<slot> already set from a first swing this page.
*temp roa_candidate combat_oa_candidate
*set combat_oa_candidate 0
*if (roa_candidate > 0)
  *set {(("combat_enemy" & roa_candidate) & "_oa_used")} true
  *set combat_active_target roa_candidate
  *gosub fight_sync_target_in
  *comment An opportunity attack is a reaction to movement, so it lands before the player has done anything else
  *comment on this page -- including picking a new target. The sync above is what makes that safe.
  *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_enemy_attack
  *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_enemy_turn "enemy" roa_candidate
*return

*label resolve_opportunity_attack_on_ally
*comment Ally-side rows 3-4 mirror (2026-09-29, on request). Resolves the swing find_opportunity_attacker picked,
*comment targeting a RETREATING ALLY instead of the player. The finder's own eligibility rules (living, melee
*comment weapon_type, range 0, oa_used unspent) don't depend on who's leaving, only on which enemy has a reaction
*comment spare -- so unlike the reverse-OA pair below (which needed a genuinely new finder, find_opportunity_
*comment attacker_against, because ITS eligibility rules differ by reactor kind), find_opportunity_attacker itself
*comment is reused completely unchanged. Only the resolver differs, calling resolve_enemy_attack_on_ally/
*comment fight_narrate_ally_attack instead of resolve_opportunity_attack's player-target versions -- same shape as
*comment the ordinary interception call site (combat_next_turn), which this is really just a delayed reaction
*comment version of.
*params roaa_ally_slot
*temp roaa_candidate combat_oa_candidate
*set combat_oa_candidate 0
*if (roaa_candidate > 0)
  *set {(("combat_enemy" & roaa_candidate) & "_oa_used")} true
  *set combat_active_target roaa_candidate
  *gosub fight_sync_target_in
  *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_enemy_attack_on_ally roaa_ally_slot
  *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "enemy" roaa_candidate "ally" roaa_ally_slot combat_enemy_atk_on_ally_hit
*return

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment COMBAT: REVERSE OPPORTUNITY ATTACKS (2026-09-29, Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §4.5, Phase 5)
*comment The reaction asymmetry closed: an enemy that Retreats can now be punished, the same way the player
*comment already can be by find_opportunity_attacker/resolve_opportunity_attack above. Two-part split (finder,
*comment then one resolver per reactor type) mirrors that pair's own shape exactly.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*label find_opportunity_attacker_against
*comment Sets combat_oa_against_kind ("player"/"ally"/"") and combat_oa_against_slot (0 for player, else ally
*comment slot) -- who punishes the enemy CURRENTLY dispatching as it opens the tier to retreat. Must be called
*comment BEFORE that enemy's own range changes, while combat_enemy_range/_ac are still this specific enemy's
*comment pre-move values (already synced by fight_sync_target_in earlier this turn, same precondition
*comment find_intercepting_ally and the quick-draw/chase logic already rely on).
*comment
*comment PLAYER FIRST, mirroring find_opportunity_attacker's own candidate rules in reverse: a melee or finesse
*comment weapon (RAW -- an opportunity attack is a melee attack; a bow-armed player simply never qualifies),
*comment combat_enemy_range <= 0 (this enemy is actually in the player's reach), and not(player_oa_used). Falls
*comment back to the lowest-HP eligible melee ally (same tiebreak as everywhere else in this file) only if the
*comment player itself isn't eligible -- "whoever's pool is left," per the plan's own framing, not a competition
*comment between them.
*set combat_oa_against_kind ""
*set combat_oa_against_slot 0
*if ((((weapon_type = "melee") or (weapon_type = "finesse")) and (combat_enemy_range <= 0)) and (not(player_oa_used)))
  *set combat_oa_against_kind "player"
*else
  *temp foaa_best_hp 0
  *temp foaa_i 1
  *label find_opportunity_attacker_against_loop
  *if (foaa_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
    *temp foaa_hp {(("combat_ally" & foaa_i) & "_hp")}
    *temp foaa_weapon {(("combat_ally" & foaa_i) & "_weapon_type")}
    *temp foaa_range {(("combat_ally" & foaa_i) & "_range")}
    *temp foaa_oa {(("combat_ally" & foaa_i) & "_oa_used")}
    *if ((((foaa_hp > 0) and (foaa_weapon = "melee")) and (foaa_range <= 0)) and (not(foaa_oa)))
      *if ((combat_oa_against_slot = 0) or (foaa_hp < foaa_best_hp))
        *set combat_oa_against_slot foaa_i
        *set foaa_best_hp foaa_hp
    *set foaa_i + 1
    *goto find_opportunity_attacker_against_loop
  *if (combat_oa_against_slot > 0)
    *set combat_oa_against_kind "ally"
*return

*label resolve_reverse_oa_player
*comment The player's own opportunity attack against the enemy currently retreating (combat_active_target is
*comment already that enemy -- fight_sync_target_in already ran for this slot this turn, so combat_enemy_ac etc.
*comment are already correct). No close-range disadvantage check: an opportunity attack is inherently melee, the
*comment penalty never applies regardless of the player's own primary weapon_type.
*comment
*comment Cannot reuse resolve_weapon_attack_choice for this -- it always *goto's away at the end (spending the
*comment player's own attack budget via fight_check_enemy_hp/fight_attack_spent), correct only for the player's
*comment own turn, never a mid-enemy-turn reaction. Attack roll and the finesse/two-handed/Dueling/Rage damage
*comment math are replicated here instead (mirroring that label's own melee branch exactly); the actual roll,
*comment resistance/vulnerability and HP application reuse resolve_player_weapon_hit whole, so Sneak Attack and
*comment Hex and Hunter's Mark still apply for free -- both already live inside that label, not duplicated here.
*comment
*comment Deliberately simpler than the player's own turn for flavor: a short, dedicated line rather than
*comment reusing pick_weapon_flavor's full per-weapon-type variant pools, which are only reachable through
*comment resolve_weapon_attack_choice's own setup. Caller checks for a kill (combat_enemy_hp <= 0) afterward,
*comment same convention as every other attack resolution call site in this file.
*if (weapon_type = "finesse")
  *if (str_mod > dex_mod)
    *set check_stat "str"
  *else
    *set check_stat "dex"
*else
  *set check_stat "str"
*set check_skill "Opportunity Attack"
*set check_dc combat_enemy_ac
*set check_add_prof_bonus true
*gosub_scene dice_engine roll_d20_check
*if (check_success)
  *set combat_player_dmg_type weapon_damage_type
  *if (weapon_type = "finesse")
    *set combat_player_dmg_dice_sides 4
    *if (str_mod > dex_mod)
      *set combat_player_dmg_bonus str_mod
    *else
      *set combat_player_dmg_bonus dex_mod
  *elseif (weapon_hands = "two_handed")
    *set combat_player_dmg_dice_sides 12
    *set combat_player_dmg_bonus str_mod
  *else
    *set combat_player_dmg_dice_sides 8
    *set combat_player_dmg_bonus str_mod
  *if (fighting_style = "dueling")
    *if (not(weapon_hands = "two_handed"))
      *set combat_player_dmg_bonus + 2
  *if ((character_class = "barbarian") and (is_raging))
    *if (weapon_type = "finesse")
      *if (str_mod > dex_mod)
        *set combat_player_dmg_bonus + barbarian_rage_damage_bonus
    *else
      *set combat_player_dmg_bonus + barbarian_rage_damage_bonus
  *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_player_weapon_hit
  *if (combat_enemy_hp <= 0)
    $!{combat_enemy_epithet} takes the opening and drops, cut down mid-retreat.
    *line_break
    [b][💀 ${combat_enemy_name} Defeated: ${combat_player_dmg_dealt} damage${combat_outgoing_dmg_suffix}][/b]
  *else
    You catch ${combat_enemy_epithet} pulling back, and the opening costs it.
    *line_break
    [b][🩸 Opportunity Attack Hits: ${combat_player_dmg_dealt} damage${combat_outgoing_dmg_suffix}][/b]
*else
  $!{combat_enemy_epithet} breaks away before you can make the opening count.
  *line_break
  [b][💨 Opportunity Attack Misses][/b]
*line_break
*return

*label resolve_reverse_oa_ally
*params droa_ally_slot
*comment An ally's own opportunity attack against the enemy currently retreating (2026-09-29). Reuses
*comment resolve_ally_attack and fight_narrate_ally_attack whole -- same reasoning as the player version reusing
*comment resolve_player_weapon_hit, and the enemy-side's own resolve_opportunity_attack reusing
*comment resolve_enemy_attack: a reaction should hit exactly like that combatant's normal attack does, no forked
*comment math or parallel narration path.
*gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_ally_attack droa_ally_slot combat_active_target
*gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "ally" droa_ally_slot "enemy" combat_active_target combat_ally_atk_hit
*return

*label fight_check_all_dead
*comment A bounded loop over combat_enemy_count, via indirection, instead of one hand-written branch per slot.
*set combat_all_enemies_dead true
*temp fcd_i 1
*label fight_check_all_dead_loop
*if (fcd_i <= combat_enemy_count)
  *temp fcd_hp {(("combat_enemy" & fcd_i) & "_hp")}
  *if (fcd_hp > 0)
    *set combat_all_enemies_dead false
  *set fcd_i + 1
  *goto fight_check_all_dead_loop
*return

*label find_intercepting_ally
*comment Interception (2026-09-28; broadened 2026-09-28 again; widened into full target selection 2026-09-29,
*comment Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §11.1 "Mechanism A"): sets combat_intercepting_ally to the living ally
*comment this enemy will actually attack instead of the player, or 0 for none (attack the player, unchanged
*comment default). Called by combat_next_turn's enemy branch right after fight_sync_target_in, so
*comment combat_active_target is the slot currently dispatching and combat_enemy_weapon_type is already synced to
*comment it.
*comment
*comment WHAT CHANGED (2026-09-28). The original rule required combat_ally<N>_target = combat_active_target as
*comment well -- i.e. the ally had to have picked THIS enemy AND already closed to range 0. That reads as
*comment "physically correct" but is far too strict in practice, because allies pick targets by kill-priority
*comment (fight_pick_target, lowest HP) rather than by who they happen to be standing next to. So the whole party
*comment converges on one wounded enemy, and every OTHER enemy in the fight walked straight past the tank to hit
*comment the player. The mechanic existed but almost never fired, which is why enemy targeting read as 100%
*comment player-focused.
*comment
*comment MECHANISM A (2026-09-28, widened 2026-09-29): any living ally already at range 0 is a legal target for
*comment whichever enemy is dispatching -- melee OR ranged ally alike now (was melee-only; a ranged ally can only
*comment ever be at range 0 if a scene deliberately opens her there, since nothing moves a ranged ally's own range
*comment during a fight -- see startup.txt's combat_ally<N>_range comment). Shared adjacency bucket, not real
*comment per-pair distance -- the loosest thing the 0-3 tier model can honestly express; combat_ally<N>_target
*comment cannot supply a real one (see its own *create comment).
*comment
*comment MECHANISM B (2026-09-29, new): if Mechanism A found no one already in reach, and the ACTING enemy is
*comment itself ranged, it would otherwise unconditionally default to the player -- prefer sniping a living
*comment ranged-classified ally instead, wherever she is (ranged attacks don't care about distance, so no range
*comment check here, unlike Mechanism A). An ally standing in melee reach is a more obvious, more urgent target
*comment than a hypothetical snipe, which is why B only runs when A found nothing, never as a tiebreak against it.
*comment Nothing here lets a ranged enemy target a MELEE ally outside Mechanism A's range-0 bucket -- that would
*comment need Layer 2's commit-and-chase (dynamic redirect, see RANGED_AI_PLAN.md §11.3), not this phase.
*comment
*comment Spreading the aggro (2026-09-28) is the other half of Mechanism A, and it is NOT optional. With the
*comment tiebreak gone, every enemy would otherwise match the same lowest-numbered engaged ally and three of them
*comment would pile onto slot 1 -- turning a tank into an unkillable meat wall the player never has to think
*comment about. Picking the LOWEST-HP eligible ally instead spreads the damage the same way the player side
*comment already does it (fight_pick_target is kill-priority for exactly this reason): the focus walks the allies
*comment down in sequence rather than dogpiling one. Ties fall to the lowest slot, because the comparison is a
*comment strict <. Mechanism B uses the same tiebreak, scoped to ranged allies only.
*comment
*comment combat_ally<N>_target is deliberately NOT read here. It keeps its other job -- recording which enemy an
*comment ally is locked onto, for the sidebar's "fighting <enemy>" sub-line (web/mygame/index.html) and the prose
*comment -- and is set in combat_next_turn's ally branch.
*set combat_intercepting_ally 0
*temp fia_best_hp 0
*temp fia_i 1
*label find_intercepting_ally_loop
*if (fia_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
  *temp fia_hp {(("combat_ally" & fia_i) & "_hp")}
  *temp fia_range {(("combat_ally" & fia_i) & "_range")}
  *if ((fia_hp > 0) and (fia_range <= 0))
    *if ((combat_intercepting_ally = 0) or (fia_hp < fia_best_hp))
      *set combat_intercepting_ally fia_i
      *set fia_best_hp fia_hp
  *set fia_i + 1
  *goto find_intercepting_ally_loop
*if ((combat_intercepting_ally = 0) and (combat_enemy_weapon_type = "ranged"))
  *temp fia_snipe_best_hp 0
  *temp fia_j 1
  *label find_intercepting_ally_snipe_loop
  *if (fia_j <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
    *temp fia_snipe_hp {(("combat_ally" & fia_j) & "_hp")}
    *temp fia_snipe_weapon {(("combat_ally" & fia_j) & "_weapon_type")}
    *if ((fia_snipe_hp > 0) and (fia_snipe_weapon = "ranged"))
      *if ((combat_intercepting_ally = 0) or (fia_snipe_hp < fia_snipe_best_hp))
        *set combat_intercepting_ally fia_j
        *set fia_snipe_best_hp fia_snipe_hp
    *set fia_j + 1
    *goto find_intercepting_ally_snipe_loop
*return

*label find_melee_ally_present
*comment Ally-side rows 3-4 mirror (2026-09-29, on request). Sets combat_melee_ally_present true if any living
*comment ally with weapon_type "melee" is at range 0. Deliberately does NOT check the player -- Mechanism A
*comment above (find_intercepting_ally) never redirects an enemy's attack onto the player, only onto an ally, so
*comment a melee-engaged player alone creates none of the payoff a melee ally does (design decision #5,
*comment RANGED_AI_PLAN.md: a melee ally doesn't eat the reaction, it gives whatever's chasing a ranged ally
*comment something else worth hitting instead). No exclude-slot param, unlike find_intercepting_ally's own
*comment candidate loop -- the only caller (combat_next_turn's ally branch) only ever runs this for a RANGED
*comment ally, which can never itself match weapon_type = "melee", so self-counting is structurally impossible.
*set combat_melee_ally_present false
*temp fmap_i 1
*label find_melee_ally_present_loop
*if (fmap_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
  *temp fmap_hp {(("combat_ally" & fmap_i) & "_hp")}
  *temp fmap_weapon {(("combat_ally" & fmap_i) & "_weapon_type")}
  *temp fmap_range {(("combat_ally" & fmap_i) & "_range")}
  *if (((fmap_hp > 0) and (fmap_weapon = "melee")) and (fmap_range <= 0))
    *set combat_melee_ally_present true
  *set fmap_i + 1
  *goto find_melee_ally_present_loop
*return

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment COMBAT: ALLY ATTACK TARGET SELECTION (2026-09-29, Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §11.1)
*comment The ally-side mirror of find_intercepting_ally's Mechanisms A/B above, called from combat_next_turn's
*comment ally branch in place of a bare *gosub fight_pick_target. fight_pick_target itself is NOT touched --
*comment 13 call sites share it (every player weapon/spell option, the player's own auto-target, and this one),
*comment too much blast radius to parameterize -- so this wraps it instead, adding a preference layer only this
*comment one call site needs, and falling back to fight_pick_target's own plain kill-priority scan whenever that
*comment layer finds nothing to prefer.
*comment
*comment HUD CLICK ALWAYS WINS, checked first and unconditionally -- exactly fight_pick_target's own existing
*comment precedence, which every ally already shares with the player's own attacks (a manual click focuses the
*comment WHOLE team, not just the player -- pre-existing behavior, not new). This label does not duplicate that
*comment logic; it just defers to fight_pick_target immediately whenever a click might be active, letting that
*comment routine's own liveness re-check run exactly as it always has.
*comment
*comment RANGED ally (Kess): prefers a living ranged-classified enemy anywhere on the field (no range check --
*comment ranged attacks don't care about distance, same reasoning as Mechanism B above) over
*comment fight_pick_target's plain lowest-HP-among-everyone scan. Falls back to that scan if no enemy is
*comment ranged-classified. Does NOT check whether Kess herself is personally threatened -- that's the ally-side
*comment equivalent of rows 2-4 (quick-draw/retreat/disengage), explicitly out of scope for this phase (see
*comment RANGED_AI_PLAN.md §11.5's "Ally-side rows 2-4 are still unscoped").
*comment
*comment MELEE ally (Rook): prefers the CLOSEST TIER PRESENT among living enemies (lowest combat_enemy<N>_range),
*comment kill priority as the tiebreak only within that tier -- so a nearby healthy enemy is chosen over a
*comment distant wounded one, rather than ignoring what's actually in front of the party to chase a kill
*comment somewhere else. Reads combat_enemy<N>_range as "how close to the fight's front this enemy is," the same
*comment shared-bucket reasoning as Mechanism A above -- not this specific ally's own real distance, which
*comment doesn't exist as data. Since every living enemy sits at SOME tier, this branch always finds a pick; the
*comment fight_pick_target fallback below it is unreachable in practice but kept for defensive completeness.
*comment
*comment Known limit, carried from RANGED_AI_PLAN.md §9: a melee ally's own combat_ally<N>_range (its progress
*comment toward whichever target it's approaching) is never reset when the picked target changes turn to turn --
*comment pre-existing, not introduced here (fight_pick_target's plain kill-priority already had the same gap).
*comment Layer 2's commit-and-chase is where a real reset rule lives, for the actively-redirecting case; this
*comment phase only ever picks among what's already reachable, so it never needs one.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*label select_ally_attack_target
*params saat_slot
*if (combat_player_chosen_target > 0)
  *gosub fight_pick_target
  *return
*temp saat_weapon {(("combat_ally" & saat_slot) & "_weapon_type")}
*temp saat_pref_target 0
*if (saat_weapon = "ranged")
  *temp saat_best_hp 0
  *temp saat_i 1
  *label select_ally_ranged_loop
  *if (saat_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp saat_hp {(("combat_enemy" & saat_i) & "_hp")}
    *temp saat_wt {(("combat_enemy" & saat_i) & "_weapon_type")}
    *if ((saat_hp > 0) and (saat_wt = "ranged"))
      *if ((saat_pref_target = 0) or (saat_hp < saat_best_hp))
        *set saat_pref_target saat_i
        *set saat_best_hp saat_hp
    *set saat_i + 1
    *goto select_ally_ranged_loop
*else
  *temp saat_min_range 999
  *temp saat_i 1
  *label select_ally_minrange_loop
  *if (saat_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp saat_hp {(("combat_enemy" & saat_i) & "_hp")}
    *if (saat_hp > 0)
      *temp saat_range {(("combat_enemy" & saat_i) & "_range")}
      *if (saat_range < saat_min_range)
        *set saat_min_range saat_range
    *set saat_i + 1
    *goto select_ally_minrange_loop
  *temp saat_best_hp 0
  *set saat_i 1
  *label select_ally_tierpick_loop
  *if (saat_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp saat_hp {(("combat_enemy" & saat_i) & "_hp")}
    *if (saat_hp > 0)
      *temp saat_range {(("combat_enemy" & saat_i) & "_range")}
      *if ((saat_range = saat_min_range) and ((saat_pref_target = 0) or (saat_hp < saat_best_hp)))
        *set saat_pref_target saat_i
        *set saat_best_hp saat_hp
    *set saat_i + 1
    *goto select_ally_tierpick_loop
*if (saat_pref_target > 0)
  *set combat_active_target saat_pref_target
  *gosub fight_sync_target_in
*else
  *gosub fight_pick_target
*return

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment COMBAT: COMMIT-AND-CHASE (2026-09-29, Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §11.3, Phase "6a")
*comment Enemy-side only in this pass -- the melee-ally mirror (a melee ally breaking off to chase a farther
*comment enemy caster/archer) is deliberately deferred to its own follow-up increment rather than shipped
*comment unverified alongside this. Both routines below take the acting slot as an explicit param (rec_slot/
*comment decs_slot) rather than reading combat_next_turn's own *temp cnt_slot implicitly -- same-file *gosub
*comment does share the caller's *temp scope in this engine (only *gosub_scene starts a fresh one), but this
*comment file's own linter flags a *temp referenced from a label positioned earlier in the file than its
*comment declaring line as "used before created," so an explicit param sidesteps the question entirely and
*comment matches how every OTHER caller-supplied value in this file (resolve_enemy_attack_on_ally's
*comment reao_a_slot, apply_derived_combat_bonuses's prefix, etc.) is already passed.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*label roll_enemy_chase_commit
*params rec_slot
*comment The one-shot decision. Called once, at the top of the closing-toward-the-player branch, only when this
*comment enemy hasn't already committed to (or already tried and declined) a chase this fight -- callers outside
*comment that branch never reach this, so "not already engaged with the default target" doesn't need its own
*comment check here. Scans living allies for a ranged-classified one at Close or Mid (1 or 2): Long (3) is
*comment deliberately out of reach ("she's not reachable easily," plan §11.3), and 0 is Layer 1's own job
*comment (Mechanism A already claims an ally already standing in melee -- see find_intercepting_ally). Lowest HP
*comment among eligible candidates wins, the same tiebreak every other target pick in this file uses.
*comment Per-type tunable (2026-09-29, on request): gated on combat_enemy_chase_chance > 0 -- a type that never
*comment set it (every creature type: lacedon, mire_rat, beast) skips the whole scan, the same "0 means never"
*comment shape combat_enemy_move2_chance already uses, rather than relying on the roll below happening to always
*comment fail (*rand can never return 0, so it would have anyway -- this just skips the wasted scan too).
*if (((combat_enemy_chase_target = 0) and (not(combat_enemy_chase_roll_done))) and (combat_enemy_chase_chance > 0))
  *temp recc_candidate 0
  *temp recc_candidate_range 0
  *temp recc_best_hp 0
  *temp recc_i 1
  *label roll_enemy_chase_commit_scan
  *if (recc_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
    *temp recc_hp {(("combat_ally" & recc_i) & "_hp")}
    *temp recc_weapon {(("combat_ally" & recc_i) & "_weapon_type")}
    *temp recc_range {(("combat_ally" & recc_i) & "_range")}
    *if (((recc_hp > 0) and (recc_weapon = "ranged")) and ((recc_range = 1) or (recc_range = 2)))
      *if ((recc_candidate = 0) or (recc_hp < recc_best_hp))
        *set recc_candidate recc_i
        *set recc_candidate_range recc_range
        *set recc_best_hp recc_hp
    *set recc_i + 1
    *goto roll_enemy_chase_commit_scan
  *if (recc_candidate > 0)
    *comment No tuned odds yet (RANGED_AI_PLAN.md §11.5) -- combat_enemy_chase_chance, per-type (33 for tactical
    *comment humanoids as a sensible starting point, the same pending-playtesting shape flee_hp_pct used).
    *temp recc_roll 0
    *rand recc_roll 1 100
    *if (recc_roll <= combat_enemy_chase_chance)
      *comment Bounded-by-range-and-reset-the-same-way (plan §11.3): the candidate's own range becomes this
      *comment enemy's new tracked pursuit distance -- not combat_enemy_range itself (that keeps tracking
      *comment distance to the player, untouched, so breaking off later needs no restore step at all).
      *set combat_enemy_chase_target recc_candidate
      *set combat_enemy_chase_range recc_candidate_range
      *set combat_enemy_chase_hp_snapshot combat_enemy_hp
      *set {(("combat_enemy" & rec_slot) & "_chase_target")} recc_candidate
      *set {(("combat_enemy" & rec_slot) & "_chase_range")} recc_candidate_range
      *set {(("combat_enemy" & rec_slot) & "_chase_hp_snapshot")} combat_enemy_hp
    *set combat_enemy_chase_roll_done true
    *set {(("combat_enemy" & rec_slot) & "_chase_roll_done")} true
*return

*label do_enemy_chase_step
*params decs_slot
*comment The shared movement step, called both the turn a chase is freshly committed (from the closing-toward-
*comment player branch, immediately after roll_enemy_chase_commit succeeds) and every turn after (from
*comment combat_next_turn's chase-continuation *elseif) -- same step either way. Closes ONE tier per turn toward
*comment combat_enemy_chase_target, no Dash-equivalent -- deliberately simpler than the player-closing branch's
*comment free-move-plus-dash shape: a chasing enemy trades away some of its aggressive tempo for breaking
*comment formation, which is both a reasonable in-fiction cost and avoids a third copy of that logic.
*set combat_enemy_chase_range - 1
*if (combat_enemy_chase_range < 0)
  *set combat_enemy_chase_range 0
*set {(("combat_enemy" & decs_slot) & "_chase_range")} combat_enemy_chase_range
*temp decs_ally_name {(("combat_ally" & combat_enemy_chase_target) & "_name")}
*temp decs_ally_epithet {(("combat_ally" & combat_enemy_chase_target) & "_epithet")}
*temp decs_rv 0
*rand decs_rv 1 3
*if (combat_enemy_chase_range <= 0)
  *comment Dedicated new prose (2026-09-29, on request) -- never the existing closing-toward-player lines, which
  *comment are hardcoded second-person ("right on top of YOU") and would be flatly wrong for a chase that never
  *comment involves the player at all.
  *if (decs_rv = 1)
    $!{combat_enemy_epithet} finally closes the last of the distance, and ${decs_ally_epithet} has nowhere left to retreat.
  *if (decs_rv = 2)
    A last hard burst of speed, and ${combat_enemy_epithet} is suddenly right on top of ${decs_ally_epithet}.
  *if (decs_rv = 3)
    The gap disappears, and ${combat_enemy_epithet} is finally within striking distance of ${decs_ally_epithet}.
  *line_break
  [b][🏃 ${combat_enemy_name} Closes on ${decs_ally_name}][/b]
  *line_break
  *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
  *gosub fight_pick_enemy_move
  *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_enemy_attack_on_ally combat_enemy_chase_target
  *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "enemy" decs_slot "ally" combat_enemy_chase_target combat_enemy_atk_on_ally_hit
  *comment Arrived and fought -- Layer 1's ordinary target selection takes back over from here (plan §11.3:
  *comment "it's not chasing anymore, it's fighting"), not locked onto this one ally for the rest of the fight.
  *set combat_enemy_chase_target 0
  *set {(("combat_enemy" & decs_slot) & "_chase_target")} 0
*else
  *if (decs_rv = 1)
    Breaking off from the fight in front of it, ${combat_enemy_epithet} cuts hard toward ${decs_ally_epithet}, closing the gap fast.
  *if (decs_rv = 2)
    $!{combat_enemy_epithet} shoulders past the melee and bears down on ${decs_ally_epithet}, ground vanishing underfoot.
  *if (decs_rv = 3)
    Ignoring everything else, ${combat_enemy_epithet} presses toward ${decs_ally_epithet}, closing the distance step by hard step.
  *line_break
  [b][🏃 ${combat_enemy_name} Closes on ${decs_ally_name}][/b]
  *line_break
  *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
*return

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment COMBAT: COMMIT-AND-CHASE, ALLY SIDE (2026-09-29, Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §11.3, "6a" mirror)
*comment A melee ally's version of roll_enemy_chase_commit/do_enemy_chase_step above -- same rule, same shape,
*comment but written against the ally system's own conventions rather than copied verbatim: no bare active-target
*comment scratch exists on this side (select_ally_attack_target and resolve_ally_attack already read
*comment combat_ally<N>_* directly via slot indirection, no sync step), so both routines take the acting slot as
*comment an explicit param and read/write the durable per-slot fields throughout, never a bare mirror.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*label roll_ally_chase_commit
*params rac_slot
*comment The one-shot decision. Called once, at the top of the closing-toward-its-target branch, only when this
*comment ally hasn't already committed to (or already tried and declined) a chase this fight. Scans living
*comment ENEMIES for a ranged-classified one at Close or Mid (1 or 2) -- reading combat_enemy<N>_range as the
*comment proxy for "how far back that enemy is standing," the same reuse-the-target's-own-range logic the enemy
*comment side's roll_enemy_chase_commit already relies on. Lowest HP among eligible candidates wins.
*temp rac_p (("combat_ally" & rac_slot) & "_")
*comment Per-type tunable (2026-09-29, on request) -- see roll_enemy_chase_commit's own comment for the full
*comment reasoning; Rook's own guard type sets this, a future non-tactical melee ally simply wouldn't.
*if ((({(rac_p & "chase_target")} = 0) and (not({(rac_p & "chase_roll_done")}))) and ({(rac_p & "chase_chance")} > 0))
  *temp racc_candidate 0
  *temp racc_candidate_range 0
  *temp racc_best_hp 0
  *temp racc_i 1
  *label roll_ally_chase_commit_scan
  *if (racc_i <= combat_enemy_count)
    *temp racc_hp {(("combat_enemy" & racc_i) & "_hp")}
    *temp racc_weapon {(("combat_enemy" & racc_i) & "_weapon_type")}
    *temp racc_range {(("combat_enemy" & racc_i) & "_range")}
    *if (((racc_hp > 0) and (racc_weapon = "ranged")) and ((racc_range = 1) or (racc_range = 2)))
      *if ((racc_candidate = 0) or (racc_hp < racc_best_hp))
        *set racc_candidate racc_i
        *set racc_candidate_range racc_range
        *set racc_best_hp racc_hp
    *set racc_i + 1
    *goto roll_ally_chase_commit_scan
  *if (racc_candidate > 0)
    *comment combat_ally<N>_chase_chance, per-type, same untuned-starting-point shape as the enemy side
    *comment (RANGED_AI_PLAN.md §11.5). Bounded by, and reset to, the candidate's own range -- see
    *comment roll_enemy_chase_commit's own comment for the full reasoning; identical here, just read from the
    *comment enemy-side fields instead of the ally-side ones.
    *temp racc_roll 0
    *rand racc_roll 1 100
    *if (racc_roll <= {(rac_p & "chase_chance")})
      *set {(rac_p & "chase_target")} racc_candidate
      *set {(rac_p & "chase_range")} racc_candidate_range
      *set {(rac_p & "chase_hp_snapshot")} {(rac_p & "hp")}
    *set {(rac_p & "chase_roll_done")} true
*return

*label do_ally_chase_step
*params dacs_slot
*comment The shared movement step, called both the turn a chase is freshly committed and every turn after -- same
*comment step either way. Closes ONE tier per turn toward this ally's chase target, no Dash-equivalent, same
*comment deliberate simplification as the enemy side's own do_enemy_chase_step.
*temp dacs_p (("combat_ally" & dacs_slot) & "_")
*temp dacs_target {(dacs_p & "chase_target")}
*temp dacs_range {(dacs_p & "chase_range")}
*set dacs_range - 1
*if (dacs_range < 0)
  *set dacs_range 0
*set {(dacs_p & "chase_range")} dacs_range
*set combat_attacker_name {(dacs_p & "name")}
*set combat_attacker_epithet {(dacs_p & "epithet")}
*temp dacs_enemy_name {(("combat_enemy" & dacs_target) & "_name")}
*temp dacs_enemy_epithet {(("combat_enemy" & dacs_target) & "_epithet")}
*temp dacs_rv 0
*rand dacs_rv 1 3
*if (dacs_range <= 0)
  *comment Dedicated new prose (2026-09-29) -- a genuinely different set of lines from the enemy-side pool above,
  *comment not the same wording with the variable names swapped.
  *if (dacs_rv = 1)
    $!{combat_attacker_epithet} finally runs ${dacs_enemy_epithet} down, closing the last of the gap.
  *if (dacs_rv = 2)
    The distance disappears, and ${combat_attacker_epithet} is on ${dacs_enemy_epithet} before it can retreat.
  *if (dacs_rv = 3)
    $!{combat_attacker_epithet} arrives hard, and ${dacs_enemy_epithet} has nowhere left to go.
  *line_break
  [b][🏃 ${combat_attacker_name} Closes on ${dacs_enemy_name}][/b]
  *line_break
  *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
  *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_ally_attack dacs_slot dacs_target
  *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "ally" dacs_slot "enemy" dacs_target combat_ally_atk_hit
  *comment Arrived and fought -- Layer 1's ordinary select_ally_attack_target takes back over from here, not
  *comment locked onto this one enemy for the rest of the fight. Unlike the enemy side's own chase (which only
  *comment ever attacks an ally, never changing the enemy count), this attack CAN kill an enemy, so the usual
  *comment death/victory check applies here.
  *set {(dacs_p & "chase_target")} 0
  *gosub fight_check_all_dead
  *if (combat_all_enemies_dead)
    *goto fight_victory
*else
  *if (dacs_rv = 1)
    $!{combat_attacker_epithet} peels away from the melee, driving hard toward ${dacs_enemy_epithet} instead.
  *if (dacs_rv = 2)
    Choosing a different fight, ${combat_attacker_epithet} bulls past what's in front of it and closes on ${dacs_enemy_epithet}.
  *if (dacs_rv = 3)
    $!{combat_attacker_epithet} breaks formation and cuts a straight line for ${dacs_enemy_epithet}, closing fast.
  *line_break
  [b][🏃 ${combat_attacker_name} Closes on ${dacs_enemy_name}][/b]
  *line_break
  *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
*return

*label fight_victory
*line_break
*set combat_enemy_hp 0
*set combat_outcome "victory"
*gosub fight_cleanup
*return

*label fight_rescued
*set hp_current 1
*set combat_outcome "rescued"
*gosub fight_cleanup
*return

*label fight_fled
*set combat_outcome "fled"
*gosub fight_cleanup
*return

*label fight_player_died
*comment Only reachable when this fight set combat_player_can_die true AND
*comment hp_current actually hit 0. Doesn't *goto_scene death itself --
*comment that would fire from inside this *gosub_scene call and corrupt the
*comment subscene stack (see narrative_guidelines.md Section 13). Instead
*comment reports combat_outcome exactly like every other exit and *returns;
*comment the CALLER checks it after the *gosub_scene returns and does the
*comment *goto_scene death death_screen itself, setting death_cause "combat"
*comment first. Every call site (*gosub_scene combat fight_<name>) needs
*comment this check even for fights that are non-lethal today, in case a
*comment later edit flips combat_player_can_die true on it.
*set combat_outcome "player_died"
*gosub fight_cleanup
*return

*comment ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
*comment THE TURN BUDGET
*comment The player has three budgets each round: combat_player_actions_left, combat_player_bonus_left and
*comment combat_player_attacks_left, each refilled from its own _max (startup.txt, all 1 by default). A round is
*comment the player's turn plus the enemies' turns today; when the fight becomes turn-ordered these become the
*comment acting creature's budget and combat_begin_round becomes combat_begin_turn.
*comment   ATTACK (the weapon/sidearm options): gated on combat_player_attacks_left > 0, left through
*comment     fight_attack_spent, which spends one attack and, only on the FIRST attack of the round, also spends
*comment     one action (Attack IS your action; Extra Attack, a future level-5 feature, lets combat_player_
*comment     attacks_max go above 1 so several swings come out of that one action instead of several actions).
*comment   ACTION (a damage spell, Agathys, False Life, Brace, an item): leaves the hub through fight_action_spent,
*comment     which spends one action.
*comment   BONUS ACTION (Hex, Healing Word, Second Wind, Rage): gated on combat_player_bonus_left > 0 (plus its
*comment     own combat_can_X availability flag, below) and left through fight_bonus_spent, which spends one.
*comment   REACTION: not a budget yet. Shield is an "armed" flag that fires on the next hit, which is how a reaction behaves.
*comment None of the three spends ends the turn by itself -- every spend just returns to the hub, regardless of
*comment what's left. The hub itself auto-ends the turn once the action and attack budgets are gone AND nothing
*comment bonus-gated is available either (see combat_bonus_available below) -- a new bonus-action feature needs
*comment its own combat_can_X flag added there, same as Hex/Healing Word/Second Wind/Rage, or it silently blocks
*comment auto-end from ever firing while it's on cooldown. The always-available "End Turn" option in the hub's
*comment own *choice block remains the only way to end the turn early, on request, with resources still unspent.
*comment Extras: an ongoing effect changes a _max value (Haste: combat_player_actions_max 2, back to 1 when it
*comment ends; its extra action is restricted in 5e to one weapon attack, Dash, Disengage, Hide or Use an Object,
*comment which a spell option can check with a flag when it is built). A one-off extra is *gosub combat_grant_
*comment action, combat_grant_bonus or combat_grant_attack (Action Surge, etc.).
*comment Every spend and grant is replay-locked (the page is replayed when the Stats screen closes), and
*comment combat_begin_round is a set of fixed values, so it is safe to repeat.
*comment ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

*label combat_begin_round
*comment Opportunity attacks (2026-09-28): each enemy regains its one reaction for the new round, exactly like
*comment the advantage/disadvantage reset below -- same per-slot loop, and deliberately NOT the round-start
*comment player_movement_restricted clear, which is a condition expiring rather than a budget refreshing.
*temp cbr_oa_i 1
*label combat_begin_round_oa_loop
*if (cbr_oa_i <= combat_enemy_count)
  *set {(("combat_enemy" & cbr_oa_i) & "_oa_used")} false
  *set cbr_oa_i + 1
  *goto combat_begin_round_oa_loop
*comment Reaction asymmetry closed (2026-09-29, plan §4.5/Phase 5): the player and every ally regain their own
*comment reaction the same way, same round boundary.
*set player_oa_used false

  *set cbr_a_oa_i + 1
  *goto combat_begin_round_ally_oa_loop
*set combat_player_disengaged false
*set combat_hide_checked false
*comment Start of a round: refill the budgets. Enemy disadvantage/advantage (Ray of Frost, Vicious Mockery, Brace,
*comment pre-fight ambush) is no longer cleared here (2026-09-28) -- now that it's stored per-slot
*comment (combat_enemy<N>_disadvantage/_advantage, see fight_sync_target_in/apply_enemy_type/resolve_enemy_attack),
*comment it's tied to that specific enemy's own next attack roll, RAW, and is consumed there instead -- a fight
*comment where the targeted enemy spends a round or two just closing distance (the range-tier system) no longer
*comment loses the debuff before that enemy ever actually attacks. player_movement_restricted stays round-scoped
*comment on purpose, for its own separate reason -- see its own create comment (startup.txt).

*label blade_ward_tick
*comment Advances Blade Ward's "until the end of your next turn" by ONE player turn. Called from
*comment fight_end_player_turn only, which is the whole point: combat_next_turn itself recurses once per
*comment COMBATANT (the enemy branch *goto's back into it at the bottom of the label), so a tick placed
*comment there would fire again after every single enemy attack and the ward would evaporate before the
*comment second enemy ever swung. Survived-then-clear gives the RAW span exactly: the first player
*comment turn-end after the cast sets survived, the second one clears.
*comment A turn lost to a stun still counts as a turn (player_stunned routes through the same shim) --
*comment agreed house rule, and the conservative reading.
*comment Replay-locked on the page_id idiom: an unguarded *set here re-runs on page replay and would
*comment expire the ward a full turn early.
*if (not(blade_ward_tick_locked)) or (not(locked_blade_ward_tick_page_id = page_id))
  *if (blade_ward_active)
    *if (blade_ward_survived_a_turn)
      *set blade_ward_active false
    *else
      *set blade_ward_survived_a_turn true
  *set blade_ward_tick_locked true
  *set locked_blade_ward_tick_page_id page_id
*return

*label fight_end_player_turn
*comment The ONE place a player turn legitimately ends, so the tick that ages Blade Ward has exactly one
*comment caller. Reached from the stun skip, the "nothing left to spend" auto-end, and the End your turn
*comment option. Deliberately NOT the fight's opening dispatch (fight_round_hub's combat_turn_needs_advance
*comment branch, where no turn has happened yet) and NOT the enemy branch's own recursion.
*gosub blade_ward_tick
*goto combat_next_turn

*label combat_spend_attack

*if (not(combat_attack_spend_locked)) or (not(locked_combat_attack_spend_page_id = page_id))
  *if (combat_player_attacks_left = combat_player_attacks_max)
    *gosub combat_spend_action
  *if (combat_player_attacks_left > 0)

*comment Cunning Action (2026-10-01, built as a level 1 house rule, then moved back to 5e's level 2 the same day): a level 2+ rogue can Dash, Disengage or Hide as a

*comment the action budget to the bonus budget (label, gate and spend all read it). With the bonus spent a rogue falls back to

*comment Nearest-eligible range per direction (2026-09-28, button projection): which tier the move will act on,
*comment so the option text can state the result outright ("Close -> Engaged") instead of leaving the player to
*comment do the arithmetic off the sidebar's range tag themselves.
*comment Deliberately does NOT call fight_pick_nearest_target to work this out. That label is the right authority
*comment for actually performing the move, but it cannot serve as a read-only preview: it overwrites
*comment combat_active_target and re-syncs the scratch the weapon-attack gates at the top of this choice have
*comment already read, and its *else clears combat_player_chosen_target when the clicked slot is alive but
*comment ineligible for that direction -- so previewing "push" while your clicked target is Engaged would
*comment silently drop that click and change who the next attack goes to. The eligibility rules are mirrored here
*comment as plain arithmetic over the slots instead, and the option bodies still call fight_pick_nearest_target
*comment as the single authority that performs the move.
*comment The chosen-target precedence is the subtle part and must match that label exactly: when
*comment combat_player_chosen_target is alive AND eligible, fight_pick_nearest_target takes it and SKIPS the
*comment nearest-scan outright (guarded by `*if (combat_active_target = 0)`). Taking the minimum anyway would
*comment mislabel the button whenever a clicked target sat at a wider tier than some other enemy -- e.g. clicking
*comment the Long Range archer while a swordsman is Close reads as pushable-from-Close, when the move that actually
*comment fires is on the archer. So the *_lock temps freeze the projection at the chosen target's tier and stop
*comment the scan from touching that direction, exactly reproducing the skip.
*comment Sentinel is 4, one above the widest real tier (3), so it can never collide with a live range value and
*comment the projection guard is a plain `< 4` rather than a negative-literal comparison.

*temp cmc_chosen_fall 4
*temp cmc_lock_push false
*temp cmc_lock_fall false
*comment Fresh distance to the slot the NEXT action will actually target, resolved HERE (before the *choice) so
*comment reach-gated options have a current number to test. The shared combat_enemy_range scratch cannot be used
*comment for that: fight_round_hub calls fight_pick_target (which sets combat_active_target) but not
*comment fight_sync_target_in, so combat_enemy_range still holds whatever the last sync left there -- a fight opened
*comment at Long Range read 0 on its first page, which offered a TOUCH spell against a foe three tiers away. Same
*comment staleness the movement projection above already had to work around for the same reason.
*comment combat_active_target is what fight_pick_target resolves to on the next action, so that is the slot to read.
*comment Falls back to combat_enemy_range when no target resolves, so a gate is never tested against 0 by default.

  *if (combat_active_target <= combat_enemy_count)

  *temp cmc_chosen_slot combat_player_chosen_target
  *if (cmc_chosen_slot <= combat_enemy_count)

      *temp cmc_chosen_range {(("combat_enemy" & cmc_chosen_slot) & "_range")}

*label combat_movement_check_loop

  *temp cmc_hp {(("combat_enemy" & cmc_i) & "_hp")}

  *set cmc_i + 1
  *goto combat_movement_check_loop
*comment Turn each direction's from/to pair into display text. combat_range_name writes the single shared

*comment Auto-end: silently loops back to the queue once there's truly nothing left to spend a resource on, not
*comment just once every pool counter happens to read zero -- attacks_left and bonus_left both sit at their full
*comment value on a turn that never touched them (a caster who casts instead of swinging, or any turn where no
*comment bonus feature got used), so testing combat_can_attack and combat_bonus_available instead of the raw
*comment counters is what tells "full because unavailable" apart from "full because unspent." Movement
*comment (2026-09-28) needs no such distinction -- unlike a bonus action, moves_left > 0 is ALWAYS usable: with
*comment two or more enemies at different ranges, at least one of Push Forward/Fall Back is always legal for SOME
*comment living enemy (proven: an enemy can't be simultaneously at range 0 and range 3), so a plain
*comment moves_left <= 0 check is sufficient. Skips past "Use an item" (not pool-gated) -- use it before running
*comment the tank dry. "Break off and run" was also skipped by that reasoning while it cost nothing at all
*comment (2026-09-28); it now costs a movement tier like any other departure and provokes an opportunity attack, so
*comment the same plain moves_left <= 0 check covers it and no separate flag is needed in that *if. Disengage is
*comment gated on the ACTION budget instead, which keeps the turn alive on its own -- and correctly so, since the
*comment *if already refuses to auto-end while actions_left > 0.

    # [b][@{combat_cunning BONUS ACTION|ACTION}][/b] [b][Disengage][/b] Move without provoking a strike for the rest of this round.
      *comment DISENGAGE (2026-09-28). 5e makes this a 3rd-level Battle Master maneuver; there is no Battle Master
      *comment in this game, and building a whole class-resource system for one maneuver is not worth it, so it is
      *comment a universal ACTION instead -- which drops straight into the existing budget (combat_player_actions_left)
      *comment and needs no new economy of any kind. The cost is the interesting part: retreat now provokes an
      *comment opportunity attack (Fall Back, and Break off below), so without this the answer to being caught in
      *comment melee is "you may leave, and eat a hit for it" with no way to decline. Now there is a real choice --
      *comment spend the action and leave clean, or keep the action and take your chances.
      *comment Covers the WHOLE round rather than one move, matching 5e's "your movement doesn't provoke opportunity
      *comment attacks" for the turn: Dash afterwards is still covered. Once per round, and the round boundary is
      *comment combat_begin_round, which clears it alongside the enemies' own reaction flags.
      *set combat_player_disengaged true
      You shift your weight off the line and square up, refusing to give anyone a clean angle on you.
      *line_break
      [b][🌀 Disengaged — nothing can strike you as you move for the rest of this round][/b]
      *line_break
      *if (combat_cunning)
        *goto fight_bonus_spent
      *goto fight_action_spent

  *if (combat_can_break_off)
    # Break off and run, out of the fight for good.
      *comment Renamed from "Break off and disengage" (2026-09-28) purely because the new Disengage action above
      *comment now owns that word and two different options answering to it is worse than a reword.
      *comment
      *comment No longer free (2026-09-28). It used to cost nothing at all -- no action, no movement -- and simply
      *comment jumped to fight_fled, which made running away strictly better than fighting. It now costs one
      *comment movement tier, like any other departure, and provokes an opportunity attack from anything that was
      *comment in reach: leaving reach is leaving reach whether you intend to keep fighting or not, and 5e is
      *comment explicit that it is. It is NOT suppressed by Disengage above -- that says your movement doesn't
      *comment provoke, and this is still movement -- so a player surrounded by melee has a real decision here:
      *comment eat the swing to escape, or accept you can't break contact on your own.
      *if (not(combat_player_disengaged))
        *gosub find_opportunity_attacker
      *gosub combat_shift_all_ranges "fallback"
      *label break_off_resolve_oa
      *gosub resolve_opportunity_attack
      *goto fight_fled

  # End your turn.
    *comment The only thing that actually ends the player's turn -- see THE TURN BUDGET above. Always offered,
    *comment whether or not anything is left to spend, so it stays correct as new resource types get added.
    *comment Resets combat_turn_advanced_this_page so next round's page-break gating starts fresh.
    *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page false
    *goto fight_end_player_turn

*comment Per-slot replay lock (2026-09-30, real bug caught live via refresh_fuzz_test.js): a bare *rand with no
*comment guard at all, re-rolled on every refresh -- the exact "dice-reroll-on-refresh" class that tool's own
*comment header names. This picks which attack variant (and dmg_type/condition) an enemy uses, so a reroll could
*comment silently change a hit into a miss or swap the damage type on nothing more than a Stats round trip. Same
*comment per-slot shape as combat_enemy_atk_locked_N/combat_enemy_narrate_locked_N (this file/combat_dice.txt) --
*comment several enemies can act within one page, so a single shared flag would suppress the SECOND enemy's own
*comment roll, not just protect against a replay. Caches only the boolean OUTCOME, not the derived fields it sets
*comment -- those are stable per-slot data, so re-deriving them from the cached boolean is exact, not an
*comment approximation.
