*comment coverage walk reaches this label with combat_active_target still at its 0 default along at least one
*comment path (found live: "Non-existent variable 'combat_enemy_move2_locked_0'", slot 0 was never *create'd
*comment since it isn't a real enemy slot). Falls back to a plain, unlocked roll for that edge case, matching
*comment this label's own pre-fix behavior there -- never reached by a real turn dispatch (fight_sync_target_in
*comment always sets a real slot first), so it doesn't need the replay protection a real slot does.
*set combat_enemy_move2_used false
*if (combat_enemy_move2_chance > 0)
  *set combat_enemy_inflicts_condition false
  *set combat_enemy_condition_name ""
  *set combat_enemy_condition_icon ""
  *if (combat_active_target > 0)
    *temp fpm_lockname (("combat_enemy_move2_locked_" & combat_active_target))
    *if (not(locked_combat_enemy_move2_page_id = page_id))
      *set combat_enemy_move2_locked_1 false
      *set combat_enemy_move2_locked_2 false
      *set combat_enemy_move2_locked_3 false
      *set combat_enemy_move2_locked_4 false
      *set combat_enemy_move2_locked_5 false
      *set combat_enemy_move2_locked_6 false
      *set locked_combat_enemy_move2_page_id page_id
    *temp fpm_already_locked {fpm_lockname}
    *temp fpm_vname (("combat_enemy" & combat_active_target) & "_move2_picked")
    *if (fpm_already_locked)
      *set combat_enemy_move2_used {fpm_vname}
    *else
      *temp fpm_roll 0
      *rand fpm_roll 1 100
      *set combat_enemy_move2_used (fpm_roll <= combat_enemy_move2_chance)
      *set {fpm_vname} combat_enemy_move2_used
      *set {fpm_lockname} true
  *else
    *temp fpm_roll 0
    *rand fpm_roll 1 100
    *set combat_enemy_move2_used (fpm_roll <= combat_enemy_move2_chance)
  *if (combat_enemy_move2_used)
    *set combat_enemy_atk_bonus combat_enemy_move2_atk_bonus
    *set combat_enemy_dmg_dice_sides combat_enemy_move2_dmg_dice_sides
    *set combat_enemy_dmg_bonus combat_enemy_move2_dmg_bonus
    *set combat_enemy_dmg_type combat_enemy_move2_dmg_type
    *set combat_enemy_inflicts_condition combat_enemy_move2_inflicts_condition
    *set combat_enemy_condition_name combat_enemy_move2_condition_name
    *set combat_enemy_condition_icon combat_enemy_move2_condition_icon
*return

*label combat_hidden_enemy_turn
*comment One enemy's turn against a hidden player (2026-10-01). Reads the active slot's scratch fields, like every enemy-turn
*comment branch. The enemy never attacks the player: a melee type closes one tier toward where it last saw them (no Dash, no
*comment attack at the end), a ranged type holds and searches. It loses the player if it ends its own move at Close or
*comment Engaged (no roll), and otherwise the player rolls ONE Stealth check per round, DC 15, when an enemy has moved or
*comment any living enemy is at Close or Engaged (disadvantage then, for being that near). The one check is spent by the first
*comment enemy that triggers it, so a group does not mean a roll each; combat_begin_round clears it.
*temp chet_moved false
*if ((not(combat_enemy_weapon_type = "ranged")) and (combat_enemy_range > 0))
  *set combat_enemy_range - 1
  *set {(("combat_enemy" & combat_active_target) & "_range")} combat_enemy_range
  *set chet_moved true
*gosub combat_range_name combat_enemy_range
*gosub_scene combat_prose flavor_hidden_enemy_turn chet_moved
*line_break
*if (chet_moved)
  [b][🏃 ${combat_enemy_name} searches, closing to ${combat_range_display_name}][/b]
  *line_break
*else
  [b][🔎 ${combat_enemy_name} searches for you][/b]
  *line_break
*if ((chet_moved) and (combat_enemy_range <= 1))
  *set combat_player_hidden false
  *gosub_scene combat_prose flavor_hidden_found_auto
  *line_break
  [b][👁️ ${combat_enemy_name} spots you][/b]
  *line_break
*elseif (not(combat_hide_checked))
  *gosub combat_check_hide
  *if ((chet_moved) or (combat_hide_blocked))
    *set combat_hide_checked true
    *if (combat_hide_blocked)
      *set disadvantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 15 "stealth" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      *gosub_scene combat_prose flavor_hidden_holds
      *line_break
      [b][👤 Still hidden][/b]
      *line_break
    *else
      *set combat_player_hidden false
      *gosub_scene combat_prose flavor_hidden_found_check
      *line_break
      [b][👁️ ${combat_enemy_name} spots you][/b]
      *line_break
*set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
*return

*label combat_next_turn
*comment The turn queue's dispatcher: walks combat_turn_kind_*/_slot_* (startup.txt) one combatant at a time, in
*comment the order combat_roll_initiative built. A dead enemy slot, or an "ally" entry (no ally AI exists yet), is
*comment a silent no-op that just advances past it -- same observable behavior as a defeated slot always having
*comment its turn skipped. Reached three ways: fight_setup's very first *goto fight_round_hub (every fight_<name>
*comment label already ends with this, unedited -- see fight_round_hub's own preamble below), the "End Turn"
*comment option, and the player_stunned check in fight_round_hub (the player's turn skipped outright).
*comment Wrapping past the last position starts a new round: combat_round + 1 (replay-locked) and
*comment combat_begin_round (refills the player's budgets, clears enemy disadvantage) -- triggered by "the index
*comment wrapped," which can only ever fire once per page since the loop always stops the instant it reaches the
*comment player.
*comment combat_turn_advanced_this_page (set true the instant anything is actually narrated -- an enemy's attack,
*comment or the player_stunned banner) gates the page_break right before the player's menu reappears, so a chain
*comment that dispatches straight to the player with nothing having happened yet (round 1's first entry, most
*comment commonly) reads with no break at all, exactly as it always did.
*set combat_current_turn_index + 1
*if (combat_current_turn_index > combat_turn_count)
  *set combat_current_turn_index 1
  *if (not(stat_bump_locked)) or (not(locked_stat_bump_page_id = page_id))
    *set combat_round + 1
    *set stat_bump_locked true
    *set locked_stat_bump_page_id page_id
  *gosub combat_begin_round
*temp cnt_kind {("combat_turn_kind_" & combat_current_turn_index)}
*temp cnt_slot {("combat_turn_slot_" & combat_current_turn_index)}
*if (cnt_kind = "player")
  *comment Reckless Attack lasts until the start of the player's next turn, which is right here.
  *set combat_reckless_active false
  *gosub fight_pick_target
  *if (combat_turn_advanced_this_page)
    *page_break Hold your ground...
  *goto fight_round_hub
*if (cnt_kind = "enemy")
  *temp cnt_hp {(("combat_enemy" & cnt_slot) & "_hp")}
  *if (cnt_hp > 0)
    *comment Turn divider (2026-09-28): a light rule before every acting combatant's own turn, so a busier
    *comment fight (several enemies/allies in one round) reads as a sequence of distinct turns instead of one
    *comment run-on block -- readability request from a live multi-combatant transcript. Printed here, at the
    *comment top of a LIVING slot's dispatch, so a dead slot's silent skip (no comment above lied) still prints
    *comment nothing at all.
    ────────────────────
    *line_break
    *set combat_active_target cnt_slot
    *gosub fight_sync_target_in
    *gosub find_intercepting_ally
    *comment Surprise round (5e RAW: a surprised creature takes no action and no move on its first turn in the
    *comment order). combat_turn_first_pass_* (startup.txt) tracks whether THIS queue position has ever dispatched
    *comment before -- read once, then cleared unconditionally, so an enemy that wins this check only loses its
    *comment very first turn and fights normally every round after. combat_player_surprise is set by the calling
    *comment scene alongside the player's own "advantage true" for a successful sneak (alderford.txt's silt-lurker
    *comment and mire-rat approaches); it does nothing here for a pre-fight buff like the camp whetstone prep, which
    *comment never touches this flag.
    *comment Range: a melee-only enemy that isn't at range 0 spends its whole turn closing one tier, same as the
    *comment player's own Push Forward -- never straight to Engaged, so an enemy opened at Mid-Range or Long
    *comment Range costs it multiple turns too.
    *temp cnt_first_pass {("combat_turn_first_pass_" & combat_current_turn_index)}
    *temp cnt_slowed {(("combat_enemy" & cnt_slot) & "_slowed")}
    *set {("combat_turn_first_pass_" & combat_current_turn_index)} false
    *comment cnt_rv: shared by both branches below that roll a 3-way flavor variant, declared once here instead
    *comment of once per branch.
    *temp cnt_rv 0
    *if (combat_player_surprise) and (cnt_first_pass)
      *comment Three variants, via *rand -- none lead with combat_enemy_epithet (usually a lowercase "the X"),
      *comment so the printed sentence never opens on an uncapitalized word.
      *rand cnt_rv 1 3
      *if (cnt_rv = 1)
        Still unaware of you, ${combat_enemy_epithet} gives no ground and finds no opening this turn.
      *if (cnt_rv = 2)
        You move before ${combat_enemy_epithet} ever marks you, and the whole turn slips by unspent.
      *if (cnt_rv = 3)
        Caught flat-footed, ${combat_enemy_epithet} loses the turn outright, never sensing you at all.
      *line_break
      [b][😲 ${combat_enemy_name} Surprised — turn lost][/b]
      *line_break
      *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
    *elseif ((((combat_enemy_weapon_type = "ranged") and (combat_enemy_range <= 0)) and (combat_intercepting_ally > 0)) and (combat_enemy_can_retreat))
      *comment Rows 3-4: a melee ally is already in the shared adjacency bucket (combat_intercepting_ally,
      *comment computed above by find_intercepting_ally) -- normally that claims this enemy's turn outright, the
      *comment *elseif just below. A cornered RANGED type that opted in (combat_enemy_can_retreat) preempts that
      *comment instead, and picks between two responses by its own current wounds (2026-09-29, plan §3/§4.5-4.7,
      *comment Phases 5-6): cross-multiplied rather than divided, to sidestep this engine's integer-only /
      *comment (GAMEPLAY_MECHANICS_RULES.md's own note on modulo/round() applies equally to a raw percentage
      *comment comparison).
      *if ((combat_enemy_hp * 100) <= (combat_enemy_max_hp * combat_enemy_flee_hp_pct))

        *comment it entirely, unlike ordinary movement) and gets no shot this turn either -- the whole turn is

        *if (cnt_rv = 3)

      *else

        *comment page. Skip the rest of this branch entirely rather than narrating a dead enemy falling back.

          *line_break

          *gosub fight_pick_enemy_move
          *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_enemy_attack
          *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_enemy_turn
          *if (combat_player_can_die) and (hp_current <= 0)
            *goto fight_player_died

          *gosub fight_check_all_dead
          *if (combat_all_enemies_dead)
            *goto fight_victory
    *elseif (combat_intercepting_ally > 0)
      *comment Interception (2026-09-28; broadened later the same day): a living melee ally is in melee range --

    *temp cnt_ar_range {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_range")}
    *temp cnt_ar_can_retreat {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_can_retreat")}
    *gosub find_melee_ally_present
    *if (cnt_a_chase_target > 0)
      *comment Commit-and-chase continuation, ally side (2026-09-29, plan §11.3, "6a" mirror) -- checked before
      *comment select_ally_attack_target even runs, since an active chase overrides its ordinary pick entirely.
      *set combat_attacker_name {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_name")}
      *set combat_attacker_epithet {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_epithet")}
      *temp cnt_a_chase_target_hp {(("combat_enemy" & cnt_a_chase_target) & "_hp")}
      *temp cnt_a_chase_hp_snapshot {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_chase_hp_snapshot")}
      *if (cnt_a_chase_target_hp <= 0)
        *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_chase_target")} 0
        $!{combat_attacker_epithet} eases off, the target already down, and looks for someone else to fight.
        *line_break
        [b][${combat_attacker_name} Breaks Off Its Chase (target down)][/b]
        *line_break
        *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
      *elseif (cnt_a_hp < cnt_a_chase_hp_snapshot)
        Hurt, ${combat_attacker_epithet} lets the chase go and turns to deal with the more immediate threat.
        *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_chase_target")} 0
        *line_break
        [b][${combat_attacker_name} Breaks Off Its Chase (took a hit)][/b]
        *line_break
        *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
      *else
        *gosub do_ally_chase_step cnt_slot
    *elseif ((((cnt_ar_weapon = "ranged") and (cnt_ar_range <= 0)) and (cnt_ar_can_retreat)) and (combat_melee_ally_present))
      *comment Ally-side rows 3-4 mirror (2026-09-29, on request): a cornered ranged ally (weapon_type "ranged",
      *comment own range <= 0) that opted in (combat_ally<N>_can_retreat) with a melee ally already at range 0 to
      *comment give whatever's threatening her something else to hit (combat_melee_ally_present, computed above)
      *comment picks between falling back cleanly or bailing out entirely, gated on her own wounds -- same
      *comment cross-multiplied percentage comparison as the enemy side (this engine's / and round() have no floor,
      *comment see GAMEPLAY_MECHANICS_RULES.md), same reused flee_hp_pct shape.
      *set combat_attacker_name {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_name")}
      *set combat_attacker_epithet {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_epithet")}
      *temp cnt_ar_maxhp {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_max_hp")}
      *temp cnt_ar_flee_pct {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_flee_hp_pct")}
      *temp cnt_ar_rv 1
      *if ((cnt_a_hp * 100) <= (cnt_ar_maxhp * cnt_ar_flee_pct))
        *comment Disengage mirror: no opportunity attack at all (RAW: Disengage suppresses it entirely, a
        *comment stronger guarantee than Retreat's "provokes but the shot is worth it"), and no shot this turn --
        *comment the whole turn spent surviving, same as the enemy side's own Disengage.
        *set cnt_ar_range + 1
        *if (cnt_ar_range > 3)
          *set cnt_ar_range 3
        *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_range")} cnt_ar_range
        *gosub combat_range_name cnt_ar_range
        *rand cnt_ar_rv 1 3
        *if (cnt_ar_rv = 1)
          Hurt, ${combat_attacker_epithet} breaks off clean, giving up the shot to put real distance behind it.
        *if (cnt_ar_rv = 2)
          $!{combat_attacker_epithet} disengages, more interested in surviving the next few seconds than another shot.
        *if (cnt_ar_rv = 3)
          Too battered to press its luck, ${combat_attacker_epithet} pulls back and leaves the fight to its ally.
        *line_break
        [b][🏃💨 ${combat_attacker_name} Disengages to ${combat_range_display_name}][/b]
        *line_break
        *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
      *else
        *comment Retreat mirror: exactly one opportunity attack against HER, from whichever eligible enemy still
        *comment has a reaction spare -- find_opportunity_attacker is reused unchanged (see
        *comment resolve_opportunity_attack_on_ally's own header for why), then she still gets her normal shot
        *comment this turn if the reaction didn't kill her.
        *gosub find_opportunity_attacker
        *gosub resolve_opportunity_attack_on_ally cnt_slot
        *gosub fight_check_all_dead
        *if (combat_all_enemies_dead)
          *goto fight_victory
        *temp cnt_ar_hp_after {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_hp")}
        *if (cnt_ar_hp_after > 0)
          *comment Re-set (2026-09-29, real bug caught live): resolve_opportunity_attack_on_ally just called
          *comment fight_narrate_ally_attack for the REACTING enemy's swing, which overwrites combat_attacker_name/
          *comment _epithet with ITS name/epithet as a side effect (that label's own job, shared globally) -- without
          *comment this re-set the "Falls Back" line below would print the enemy's own name breaking off, not hers.
          *set combat_attacker_name {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_name")}
          *set combat_attacker_epithet {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_epithet")}
          *set cnt_ar_range + 1
          *if (cnt_ar_range > 3)
            *set cnt_ar_range 3
          *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_range")} cnt_ar_range
          *gosub combat_range_name cnt_ar_range
          *rand cnt_ar_rv 1 3
          *if (cnt_ar_rv = 1)
            $!{combat_attacker_epithet} breaks off, falling back a step to open a clean line.
          *if (cnt_ar_rv = 2)
            Backing away fast, ${combat_attacker_epithet} puts a step of distance between itself and the blade at its side.
          *if (cnt_ar_rv = 3)
            $!{combat_attacker_epithet} gives ground, retreating just far enough to loose a clean shot.
          *line_break
          [b][🏃 ${combat_attacker_name} Falls Back to ${combat_range_display_name}][/b]
          *line_break
          *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
          *gosub select_ally_attack_target cnt_slot
          *if (combat_active_target > 0)
            *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_target")} combat_active_target
            *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_ally_attack cnt_slot combat_active_target
            *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "ally" cnt_slot "enemy" combat_active_target combat_ally_atk_hit
            *set combat_ally_atk_used_sidearm false
            *gosub fight_check_all_dead
            *if (combat_all_enemies_dead)
              *goto fight_victory
    *else
      *gosub select_ally_attack_target cnt_slot
      *if (combat_active_target > 0)
        *comment Stored durably (not just left in the shared/transient combat_active_target) so
        *comment find_intercepting_ally can still tell, on a LATER turn (this enemy's, possibly several turn-order
        *comment positions from now), which specific enemy this ally is engaged with.
        *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_target")} combat_active_target
        *set combat_attacker_name {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_name")}
        *set combat_attacker_epithet {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_epithet")}
        *temp cnt_a_weapon {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_weapon_type")}
        *temp cnt_a_range {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_range")}
        *if ((cnt_a_weapon = "ranged") or (cnt_a_range <= 0))
          *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_ally_attack cnt_slot combat_active_target
          *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "ally" cnt_slot "enemy" combat_active_target combat_ally_atk_hit
          *comment Clear the quick-draw flag right after the narration, mirroring the enemy side's clear of
          *comment combat_enemy_used_sidearm in the same turn position. The turn-scoped rule is what keeps an archer from
          *comment being left permanently knife-armed: the durable combat_ally<N>_weapon_type was never changed, only
          *comment the flag, so next turn she reads as ranged again and the bow pool returns. Resetting it HERE rather
          *comment than inside fight_narrate_ally_attack keeps that label purely a narrator, and matters because the
          *comment enemy-as-attacker paths (interception/chase) reuse the label without clearing anything.
          *set combat_ally_atk_used_sidearm false
          *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
          *gosub fight_check_all_dead
          *if (combat_all_enemies_dead)
            *goto fight_victory
        *else
          *comment Free movement + Dash (2026-09-28, mirrors the player's own Push Forward/Dash and the enemy
          *comment branch just above): closes its OWN combat_ally<N>_range one tier for free, independent of
          *comment combat_enemy_range, so this ally can be nearer to (or farther from) the target than the player is.
          *comment Dashes (closes a second tier) if the free move alone still doesn't reach -- no attack to give up
          *comment either way this turn -- and only attacks this same turn if the free move alone was enough.
          *gosub roll_ally_chase_commit cnt_slot
          *if ({(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_chase_target")} > 0)
            *gosub do_ally_chase_step cnt_slot
          *else
            *set cnt_a_range - 1
            *if (cnt_a_range < 0)
              *set cnt_a_range 0
            *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_range")} cnt_a_range
            *temp cnt_a_dashed false
            *if (cnt_a_range > 0)
              *set cnt_a_range - 1
              *if (cnt_a_range < 0)
                *set cnt_a_range 0
              *set {(("combat_ally" & cnt_slot) & "_range")} cnt_a_range
              *set cnt_a_dashed true
            *temp cnt_arv 1
            *rand cnt_arv 1 3
            *if (cnt_a_range <= 0)
              *if (cnt_arv = 1)
                A last burst of speed closes the distance, and ${combat_attacker_epithet} is suddenly right on top of the fight.
              *if (cnt_arv = 2)
                In one hard rush, ${combat_attacker_epithet} crosses what ground was left and closes to melee range.
              *if (cnt_arv = 3)
                The last of the gap disappears fast, and ${combat_attacker_epithet} is finally within striking distance.
              *line_break
              [b][🏃 ${combat_attacker_name} Closes to Engagement@{cnt_a_dashed  (Dashed the extra distance)|}][/b]
            *else
              *gosub combat_range_name cnt_a_range
              *if (cnt_arv = 1)
                Ground vanishes underfoot as ${combat_attacker_epithet} presses in, cutting down the distance to the fight.
              *if (cnt_arv = 2)
                In a burst of motion, ${combat_attacker_epithet} closes some of the gap, cutting down what's left.
              *if (cnt_arv = 3)
                Step by step, ${combat_attacker_epithet} narrows the distance, closing in fast.
              *line_break
              [b][🏃 ${combat_attacker_name} Closes to ${combat_range_display_name}@{cnt_a_dashed  (Dashed the extra distance)|}][/b]
            *line_break
            *set combat_turn_advanced_this_page true
            *if ((cnt_a_range <= 0) and (not(cnt_a_dashed)))
              *gosub_scene combat_dice resolve_ally_attack cnt_slot combat_active_target
              *gosub_scene combat_prose fight_narrate_ally_attack "ally" cnt_slot "enemy" combat_active_target combat_ally_atk_hit
              *comment Clear the quick-draw flag right after the narration, mirroring the enemy side's clear of
              *comment combat_enemy_used_sidearm in the same turn position. The turn-scoped rule is what keeps an archer
              *comment from being left permanently knife-armed: the durable combat_ally<N>_weapon_type was never changed,
              *comment only the flag, so next turn she reads as ranged again and the bow pool returns. Resetting it HERE
              *comment rather than inside fight_narrate_ally_attack keeps that label purely a narrator, and matters
              *comment because the enemy-as-attacker paths (interception/chase) reuse the label without clearing anything.
              *set combat_ally_atk_used_sidearm false
              *gosub fight_check_all_dead
              *if (combat_all_enemies_dead)
                *goto fight_victory
*goto combat_next_turn

*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
*comment ENEMY TYPE LIBRARY
*comment A reusable alternative to hand-authoring a slot's stat block/
*comment movepool/flavor inline: *set combat_apply_type "<type>", *set
*comment combat_apply_slot <1/2/3>, *gosub apply_enemy_type. Lets a mixed
*comment fight drop the same enemy type into more than one slot (or combine
*comment different types) without forking a one-off copy of that type's
*comment numbers into a new fight label just to change which slots it fills.
*comment
*comment NAMING: a type is a WEAPON, not a person. Its lines name the fighter through
*comment combat_enemy_epithet, "$!{combat_enemy_epithet}" at the start of a sentence (the engine capitalizes it) and
*comment "${combat_enemy_epithet}" inside one, so the same swordsman lines serve a knight, a bandit, a duelist or a
*comment named captain. The epithet defaults to the type ("the swordsman") and a fight renames it: set
*comment combat_enemy_epithet_override (and combat_enemy_name_override for the banner) before the slot is built.
*comment This works for the library's lines only because they are built at the moment of the attack, by
*comment flavor_from_type, when the attacker is already the active slot: a ${var} inside a *set "..." string is
*comment resolved the instant that line runs (verified against web/scene.js replaceVariables), so a line stored at
*comment fight setup (the player's strike pools, a hand-authored fight's per-slot lines) cannot use the epithet and
*comment names its target "they" or "the creature" instead. Two copies of the same type in one fight (2 Guards) draw
*comment from the same pool of eight, rolled independently on every attack, so they do not repeat in lockstep.
*comment
*comment Optional per-instance rename: *set combat_enemy1/2/3_name_override
*comment and/or _epithet_override before calling apply_enemy_type for that
*comment slot (slot 1 instead uses the plain combat_enemy_name_override,
*comment applied later by fight_setup -- see its own comment). This DOES
*comment correctly rename the status banner, spell-targeting text, and
*comment victory/defeat lines, since those interpolate ${combat_enemy_name}/
*comment ${combat_enemy_epithet} live in directly-printed text, not through
*comment a stored variable -- and, with NAMING above, into the type's own
*comment prose too, since that names the fighter through the epithet. Consumed and cleared
*comment the moment that slot's *gosub apply_enemy_type runs (slot 1 when
*comment fight_setup runs), so it never bleeds into a later fight or a
*comment different slot reusing the same type.
*comment
*comment Available types, by weapon: "swordsman", "axeman", "knifeman", "maceman", "spearman", "hookman" (hooked
*comment pole), "archer", "slinger" and "guard" (plain mace, no special move), and the creatures "lacedon", "mire_rat" and
*comment "beast" (a generic natural-attack monster; the player's lines against them are flavor_creature_<slashing|piercing|
*comment bludgeoning|ranged>, where against the human types they are flavor_player_<...>, both built per attack by the
*comment player's weapon). A scene reaches any of them through fight_library, below, with no edit to this
*comment file. A type is stats and movepool in apply_type_<name> (one block per slot, here) plus prose in
*comment flavor_type_<name> (written once, see ENEMY TYPE FLAVOR in combat_prose.txt). Add a new one with an
*comment apply_type_<name> label here, one line in apply_enemy_type's dispatch, and the prose label with its line
*comment in flavor_from_type (combat_prose.txt).
*comment
*comment WRITING RULE: every type's hit and miss lines, and the player's apply_generic_weapon_flavor
*comment pool, are written to work in any fight against human combatants, anywhere, so a new fight can call a type
*comment with no retuning. That means no place (ground, walls, props, water, streets), no weather, lighting or
*comment time of day, nothing the player may not have on (cloak, coat, cloth, leather, boots, armor, harness), and
*comment no he, she or it for the enemy (a back-reference is singular "they", never "the body"). Each line is one physical action: how the attack moves, where it lands or how
*comment the player turns it aside, and what it costs. No fight layers place-specific lines over a type: a scene
*comment that wants a place sets the place in its own prose around the fight.
*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

*label apply_enemy_type
*if (combat_apply_type = "swordsman")
  *gosub apply_type_swordsman
*if (combat_apply_type = "axeman")
  *gosub apply_type_axeman
*if (combat_apply_type = "knifeman")
  *gosub apply_type_knifeman
*if (combat_apply_type = "maceman")
  *gosub apply_type_maceman
*if (combat_apply_type = "spearman")
  *gosub apply_type_spearman
*if (combat_apply_type = "hookman")
  *gosub apply_type_hookman
*if (combat_apply_type = "archer")
  *gosub apply_type_archer
*if (combat_apply_type = "slinger")
  *gosub apply_type_slinger
*if (combat_apply_type = "guard")
  *gosub apply_type_guard
*if (combat_apply_type = "lacedon")
  *gosub apply_type_lacedon
*if (combat_apply_type = "mire_rat")
  *gosub apply_type_mire_rat
*if (combat_apply_type = "beast")
  *gosub apply_type_beast
*comment Which library type fills this slot. fight_narrate_enemy_turn reads the slot's hit, miss and move-2 prose from
*comment flavor_from_type by this id, so each type's lines are written once (flavor_type_<name>), not once per slot.
*comment Which library type fills this slot, for any slot, via indirection (fight_narrate_enemy_turn reads it
*comment the same way to pick that slot's hit/miss prose from flavor_from_type).
*set {(("combat_enemy" & combat_apply_slot) & "_flavor_type")} combat_apply_type
*comment Pre-fight ambush advantage (2026-09-28): a scene that sets the bare combat_enemy_advantage true BEFORE
*comment calling the fight ("the creature strikes first" -- several sites in alderford.txt/port_valen.txt/
*comment port_valen_dredge_end.txt) means every enemy the fight is about to seat gets it, not just slot 1 --
*comment applied here, once per slot as each is built, so a 3-enemy ambush (fight_granary_rats) grants it to all
*comment three, not only the first. fight_cleanup clears the bare pending flag afterward so it can't leak into a
*comment later fight that never set it. Mid-fight, per-target grants (Vicious Mockery, Ray of Frost, Brace,
*comment below) work differently -- they write directly to one already-built slot's own field via
*comment combat_active_target indirection, not through this pending mechanism.
*if (combat_enemy_advantage)
  *set {(("combat_enemy" & combat_apply_slot) & "_advantage")} true
*if (combat_apply_slot = 1)
  *comment Slot 1's rename-override is applied later, by fight_setup, once the calling fight_<name> label is
  *comment done setting up every slot -- see combat_enemy_name_override's comment in startup.txt. Nothing to do
  *comment here for slot 1.
*else
  *comment Slots 2 and beyond: one generic block, via indirection, instead of a hand-written branch per slot.
  *temp aet_p (("combat_enemy" & combat_apply_slot) & "_")
  *temp aet_name_ov {(aet_p & "name_override")}
  *temp aet_epi_ov {(aet_p & "epithet_override")}
  *if (not(aet_name_ov = ""))
    *set {(aet_p & "name")} aet_name_ov
    *set {(aet_p & "name_override")} ""
  *if (not(aet_epi_ov = ""))
    *set {(aet_p & "epithet")} aet_epi_ov
    *set {(aet_p & "epithet_override")} ""
*return

*label apply_type_swordsman
*comment Sword fighter: measured cuts and thrusts, a little harder to hit (AC 12). Move 2 (30%) is a beat and a double cut
*comment that drives the player off guard (Off-Guard). Not balance-simmed.
*if (combat_apply_slot = 1)
  *set combat_enemy_name "Swordsman"
  *set combat_enemy_hp 10
  *set combat_enemy_max_hp 10
  *set combat_enemy_ac 12
  *set combat_enemy_dmg_dice_sides 6
  *set combat_enemy_epithet "the swordsman"
  *set combat_enemy_dmg_type "slashing"
  *set combat_enemy_move2_chance 30
  *set combat_enemy_move2_atk_bonus 2
  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_dice_sides 4
  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_bonus 0
  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_type "slashing"
  *set combat_enemy_move2_inflicts_condition true
  *set combat_enemy_move2_condition_name "Off-Guard"
  *set combat_enemy_move2_condition_icon "🗡️"
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "strength" 12 14
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "dexterity" 12 14
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "constitution" 12 14
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "intelligence" 8 10
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "wisdom" 9 11
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "charisma" 8 10
  *gosub apply_derived_combat_bonuses "combat_enemy_"
  *comment Tactical/human -- breaks formation to chase a squishy target sometimes (2026-09-29, on request).
  *set combat_enemy_chase_chance 33
*else
  *comment Slots 2 and beyond: one generic block, via indirection, instead of a hand-copied block per slot
  *comment (the same values as slot 1 above, by construction).
  *temp apt_p (("combat_enemy" & combat_apply_slot) & "_")
  *set {(apt_p & "name")} "Swordsman"
  *set {(apt_p & "hp")} 10
  *set {(apt_p & "max_hp")} 10
  *set {(apt_p & "ac")} 12
  *set {(apt_p & "dmg_dice_sides")} 6
  *set {(apt_p & "epithet")} "the swordsman"
  *set {(apt_p & "dmg_type")} "slashing"
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_chance")} 30
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_atk_bonus")} 2
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_dmg_dice_sides")} 4
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_dmg_bonus")} 0
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_dmg_type")} "slashing"
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_inflicts_condition")} true
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_condition_name")} "Off-Guard"
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_condition_icon")} "🗡️"
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "strength" 12 14
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "dexterity" 12 14
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "constitution" 12 14
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "intelligence" 8 10
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "wisdom" 9 11
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "charisma" 8 10
  *gosub apply_derived_combat_bonuses apt_p
  *set {(apt_p & "chase_chance")} 33
*return

*label apply_type_axeman

*comment player Bleeding (disadvantage on the next attack, like every condition-on-hit here -- the name is flavor).
*if (combat_apply_slot = 1)
  *set combat_enemy_name "Axeman"
  *set combat_enemy_hp 10
  *set combat_enemy_max_hp 10
  *set combat_enemy_ac 11

  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_dice_sides 4

  *comment Slots 2 and beyond: one generic block, via indirection, instead of a hand-copied block per slot
  *comment (the same values as slot 1 above, by construction).

*label apply_type_archer
*comment Bow fighter: fragile (7 HP, AC 10) but hits hard from range. Move 2 (25%) is a shot at the legs (Hobbled).
*comment Not balance-simmed.
*if (combat_apply_slot = 1)
  *set combat_enemy_name "Archer"
  *set combat_enemy_hp 7
  *set combat_enemy_max_hp 7
  *set combat_enemy_ac 10
  *set combat_enemy_dmg_dice_sides 6
  *set combat_enemy_epithet "the archer"
  *set combat_enemy_dmg_type "piercing"
  *set combat_enemy_weapon_type "ranged"
  *set combat_enemy_move2_chance 25
  *set combat_enemy_move2_atk_bonus 2
  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_dice_sides 4
  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_bonus 0
  *set combat_enemy_move2_dmg_type "piercing"
  *set combat_enemy_move2_inflicts_condition true
  *set combat_enemy_move2_condition_name "Hobbled"
  *set combat_enemy_move2_condition_icon "🦵"
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "strength" 9 11
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "dexterity" 15 16
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "constitution" 10 12
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "intelligence" 9 11
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "wisdom" 11 13
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range "combat_enemy_" "charisma" 8 10
  *gosub apply_derived_combat_bonuses "combat_enemy_"
  *comment Sidearm (2026-09-29, Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §4.1/§4.2): a dagger, drawn only when
  *comment combat_next_turn's row-2 decision fires (cornered, no melee ally in the shared adjacency bucket --
  *comment see find_intercepting_ally). Same DEX-based to-hit/damage bonus as the bow just above (a dagger is
  *comment finesse, same ability either way), only the die shrinks -- 1d4 piercing instead of 1d6, "weaker
  *comment damage" per the plan's own decision table (§3, row 2) -- and it takes no ranged-at-close-range
  *comment disadvantage, since a dagger swing is a melee attack.
  *set combat_enemy_sidearm_weapon_type "melee"
  *set combat_enemy_sidearm_atk_bonus combat_enemy_atk_bonus
  *set combat_enemy_sidearm_dmg_dice_sides 4
  *set combat_enemy_sidearm_dmg_bonus combat_enemy_dmg_bonus
  *set combat_enemy_sidearm_dmg_type "piercing"
  *comment Retreat (2026-09-29, plan §4.5/Phase 5): tactical humanoid, can fall back when cornered with a melee
  *comment ally to cover it -- see combat_enemy_can_retreat's own *create comment for the full rule.
  *set combat_enemy_can_retreat true
  *comment Disengage's wounded threshold (2026-09-29, plan §4.7/Phase 6): 33%, the same sensible-default-
  *comment pending-playtesting starting point every other untuned percentage in this plan used.
  *set combat_enemy_flee_hp_pct 33
*else
  *comment Slots 2 and beyond: one generic block, via indirection, instead of a hand-copied block per slot
  *comment (the same values as slot 1 above, by construction).
  *temp apt_p (("combat_enemy" & combat_apply_slot) & "_")
  *set {(apt_p & "name")} "Archer"
  *set {(apt_p & "hp")} 7
  *set {(apt_p & "max_hp")} 7
  *set {(apt_p & "ac")} 10
  *set {(apt_p & "dmg_dice_sides")} 6
  *set {(apt_p & "epithet")} "the archer"
  *set {(apt_p & "dmg_type")} "piercing"
  *set {(apt_p & "weapon_type")} "ranged"
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_chance")} 25
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_atk_bonus")} 2
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_dmg_dice_sides")} 4
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_dmg_bonus")} 0
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_dmg_type")} "piercing"
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_inflicts_condition")} true
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_condition_name")} "Hobbled"
  *set {(apt_p & "move2_condition_icon")} "🦵"
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "strength" 9 11
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "dexterity" 15 16
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "constitution" 10 12
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "intelligence" 9 11
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "wisdom" 11 13
  *gosub apply_ability_score_range apt_p "charisma" 8 10
  *gosub apply_derived_combat_bonuses apt_p
  *comment Sidearm -- see slot 1's own comment above for the full rule.
  *set {(apt_p & "sidearm_weapon_type")} "melee"
  *set {(apt_p & "sidearm_atk_bonus")} {(apt_p & "atk_bonus")}
  *set {(apt_p & "sidearm_dmg_dice_sides")} 4
  *set {(apt_p & "sidearm_dmg_bonus")} {(apt_p & "dmg_bonus")}
  *set {(apt_p & "sidearm_dmg_type")} "piercing"

  *set {(apt_p & "sidearm_dmg_type")} "piercing"

*label apply_generic_weapon_flavor
*comment The player's side of a fight against a human fighter. Nothing is stored: every weapon line, finishing blow and
*comment the Dissonant Whispers pair is built at the moment it is needed, from flavor_player_<damage type> and
*comment flavor_kill_/flavor_yield_<damage type> (PLAYER STRIKE FLAVOR and PLAYER FINISHING BLOWS, combat_prose.txt), which is what lets
*comment them follow the weapon's damage type and name the fighter through its epithet. This label only raises the flags
*comment pick_weapon_flavor and pick_weapon_death_flavor read. A hand-authored fight can still leave combat_player_keyed
*comment false and write its own combat_flavor_* lines instead.
*set combat_player_keyed true
*set combat_player_creature false
*return

*label apply_creature_weapon_flavor
*comment The player's side of a fight against a creature (a lacedon, a rat, a beast). The same as
*comment apply_generic_weapon_flavor, but pick_weapon_flavor and pick_weapon_death_flavor use the flavor_creature_ labels
*comment (PLAYER LINES AGAINST A CREATURE, combat_prose.txt), which assume only a body with a head, shoulders, flank and legs.
*set combat_player_keyed true
*set combat_player_creature true
*return

*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
*comment ALLY TYPE LIBRARY (2026-09-28)
*comment A reusable alternative to hand-authoring an ally's stat block inline, mirroring ENEMY TYPE LIBRARY below:
*comment a scene sets combat_ally_lib_1 (and optionally _2/_3/_4) to a type id before calling fight_library, and
*comment optionally combat_ally1_name_override/_epithet_override (and the _2/_3/_4 versions) to reuse a type under
*comment a unique name -- "Kess" instead of the archer type's generic "Archer" -- with no other change. Unlike
*comment enemy slot 1, every ally slot (1-4) is written directly via indirection; there's no "bare" canonical slot
*comment to special-case. combat_ally1_range (and _2/_3/_4) is deliberately untouched here -- like
*comment combat_enemy_range, it's caller-optional: leave it at the type's default (0, Engaged) or set it before
*comment calling fight_library to open a melee ally away from Engaged, same as combat_enemy_range 3 in
*comment fight_dev_range_test.
*comment Available types: "archer" and "slinger" (both ranged, keep their distance, both quick-draw a dagger when a
*comment target closes to range 0), and "guard" (melee, closes and strikes).
*comment combat_ally_lib_* is cleared by fight_cleanup.
*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

*label apply_ally_roster
*comment Called from fight_library, before fight_setup. Applies every filled combat_ally_lib_N (1..
*comment combat_ally_slot_cap) via apply_ally_type, the same bounded-loop-plus-indirection pattern fight_library's
*comment own slot loop uses for enemies, and sets combat_ally_count to the highest filled slot. A fight with no
*comment allies at all (combat_ally_lib_1 through _4 all "") leaves combat_ally_count at 0, same as ever.
*set combat_ally_count 0
*temp aar_i 1
*label apply_ally_roster_loop
*if (aar_i <= combat_ally_slot_cap)
  *temp aar_lib {("combat_ally_lib_" & aar_i)}
  *if (not(aar_lib = ""))
    *set combat_ally_count aar_i
    *set combat_apply_ally_slot aar_i
    *set combat_apply_ally_type aar_lib
    *gosub apply_ally_type
  *set aar_i + 1
  *goto apply_ally_roster_loop
*return

*label apply_ally_type
*if (combat_apply_ally_type = "archer")
  *gosub apply_allytype_archer
*if (combat_apply_ally_type = "slinger")
  *gosub apply_allytype_slinger
*if (combat_apply_ally_type = "guard")
  *gosub apply_allytype_guard
*temp aat_p (("combat_ally" & combat_apply_ally_slot) & "_")
*temp aat_name_ov {(aat_p & "name_override")}
*temp aat_epi_ov {(aat_p & "epithet_override")}
*if (not(aat_name_ov = ""))
  *set {(aat_p & "name")} aat_name_ov
  *set {(aat_p & "name_override")} ""
*if (not(aat_epi_ov = ""))
  *set {(aat_p & "epithet")} aat_epi_ov
  *set {(aat_p & "epithet_override")} ""
*return

*label apply_allytype_archer
*comment Ranged skirmisher: a bow at range, keeps its distance. resolve_ally_attack never checks range for a
*comment weapon_type "ranged" ally, so this one attacks every turn regardless of combat_ally1_range. HP/AC/damage
*comment dice stay flat authored numbers (same convention every enemy type still uses -- apply_derived_hp/_ac
*comment exist but nothing opts into them yet). atk_bonus/dmg_bonus (2026-09-28) derive from rolled ability
*comment scores through the SAME apply_ability_score_range/apply_derived_combat_bonuses the enemy library uses,
*comment replacing Kess's original fixed +4/+2 -- DEX 14-16 keeps the roll centered close to those original
*comment numbers rather than a from-scratch retune. weapon_type must be set before apply_derived_combat_bonuses
*comment runs, since it reads {prefix & "weapon_type"} to pick DEX over STR.
*temp aaa_p (("combat_ally" & combat_apply_ally_slot) & "_")
*set {(aaa_p & "name")} "Archer"
*set {(aaa_p & "epithet")} "the archer"
*set {(aaa_p & "hp")} 10
*set {(aaa_p & "max_hp")} 10
*set {(aaa_p & "ac")} 13
*set {(aaa_p & "dmg_dice_sides")} 6
*set {(aaa_p & "dmg_type")} "piercing"
*set {(aaa_p & "weapon_type")} "ranged"
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aaa_p "strength" 8 10
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aaa_p "dexterity" 14 16
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aaa_p "constitution" 10 12
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aaa_p "intelligence" 8 10
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aaa_p "wisdom" 10 12
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aaa_p "charisma" 8 10
*gosub apply_derived_combat_bonuses aaa_p
*comment Sidearm (2026-09-29, Ranged-AI Position Awareness plan §4.1, extended to allies on request -- Kess is
*comment this type, given one for the same reason the enemy archer is): a dagger, same DEX-based to-hit/damage
*comment bonus as the bow just above, only the die drops to 1d4. Read by resolve_ally_attack (combat_dice.txt),
*comment which quick-draws it for the ONE attack a ranged ally makes while a target is at range 0 -- shooting from
*comment inside a foe's reach costs disadvantage, and the knife does not. Turn-scoped, never a persistent equip.
*comment "Always fully rewrites" per this label's own header, so apply_allytype_guard clears it explicitly rather
*comment than relying on a reset loop -- there isn't one for ally fields (see fight_cleanup_ally_loop's comment).
*set {(aaa_p & "sidearm_weapon_type")} "melee"
*set {(aaa_p & "sidearm_atk_bonus")} {(aaa_p & "atk_bonus")}
*set {(aaa_p & "sidearm_dmg_dice_sides")} 4
*set {(aaa_p & "sidearm_dmg_bonus")} {(aaa_p & "dmg_bonus")}
*set {(aaa_p & "sidearm_dmg_type")} "piercing"
*comment Retreat/Disengage (2026-09-29, ally-side rows 3-4 mirror, on request): same per-type opt-in as the
*comment enemy archer/slinger. 33% max HP untuned starting point, same as the enemy side -- see
*comment RANGED_AI_PLAN.md §4.7 for why this hasn't been balance-simmed either.
*set {(aaa_p & "can_retreat")} true
*set {(aaa_p & "flee_hp_pct")} 33
*return

*label apply_allytype_slinger

*comment (combat_prose.txt) has no per-type flavor dispatch for allies -- fnaa_ranged is one generic "shot" pool
*comment shared by every ranged ally, so this reads mechanically identical to apply_allytype_archer at range (same
*comment DEX-based derived bonuses, same close-range quick-draw) and narrates the same as any other ranged ally shot.
*comment The die is smaller (1d6, not the archer's), and the HP/AC are nudged a little softer to reflect a thrown
*comment stone being a poor answer to a shield.
*temp asl_p (("combat_ally" & combat_apply_ally_slot) & "_")

*set {(asl_p & "dmg_type")} "bludgeoning"
*set {(asl_p & "weapon_type")} "ranged"
*gosub apply_ability_score_range asl_p "strength" 8 10
*gosub apply_ability_score_range asl_p "dexterity" 14 16
*gosub apply_ability_score_range asl_p "constitution" 10 12
*gosub apply_ability_score_range asl_p "intelligence" 8 10
*gosub apply_ability_score_range asl_p "wisdom" 10 12

*set {(asl_p & "sidearm_weapon_type")} "melee"
*set {(asl_p & "sidearm_atk_bonus")} {(asl_p & "atk_bonus")}
*set {(asl_p & "sidearm_dmg_dice_sides")} 4
*set {(asl_p & "sidearm_dmg_bonus")} {(asl_p & "dmg_bonus")}
*set {(asl_p & "sidearm_dmg_type")} "piercing"
*comment Retreat/Disengage: same as apply_allytype_archer's own field, same untuned 33%.
*set {(asl_p & "can_retreat")} true
*set {(asl_p & "flee_hp_pct")} 33
*return

*label apply_allytype_guard
*comment Melee bruiser: sword and shield, holds the line. First melee archetype -- exercises resolve_ally_attack's
*comment melee path end to end, gated on this ally's OWN combat_ally<N>_range (combat_next_turn's ally branch)
*comment reaching 0 before it swings. HP/AC/damage dice stay flat authored numbers, same convention as every
*comment enemy type. atk_bonus/dmg_bonus (2026-09-28) derive from rolled ability scores the same way the archer
*comment archetype's do -- STR 14-16 keeps the roll centered close to the original fixed +4/+2, and STR/DEX
*comment "whichever rolled higher" (apply_derived_combat_bonuses' own rule) never actually picks DEX here since
*comment its 10-12 range can't out-roll STR's 14-16.
*temp aag_p (("combat_ally" & combat_apply_ally_slot) & "_")
*set {(aag_p & "name")} "Guard"
*set {(aag_p & "epithet")} "the guard"

*set {(aag_p & "max_hp")} 13
*set {(aag_p & "ac")} 15
*set {(aag_p & "dmg_dice_sides")} 8
*set {(aag_p & "dmg_type")} "slashing"
*set {(aag_p & "weapon_type")} "melee"
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aag_p "strength" 14 16
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aag_p "dexterity" 10 12
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aag_p "constitution" 12 14
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aag_p "intelligence" 8 10
*gosub apply_ability_score_range aag_p "wisdom" 10 12

*gosub apply_derived_combat_bonuses aag_p

*comment explicitly rather than reset by a loop.
*set {(aag_p & "sidearm_weapon_type")} ""
*set {(aag_p & "sidearm_atk_bonus")} 0
*set {(aag_p & "sidearm_dmg_dice_sides")} 4
*set {(aag_p & "sidearm_dmg_bonus")} 0
*set {(aag_p & "sidearm_dmg_type")} "piercing"
*comment Tactical/human -- same reasoning as the enemy-side humanoid melee types (2026-09-29, on request): Rook
*comment can break off to chase a squishy enemy caster/archer sometimes.
*set {(aag_p & "chase_chance")} 33
*comment Retreat/Disengage: a melee bruiser holds the line, it doesn't fall back from one -- cleared explicitly
*comment for the same reason sidearm is cleared above rather than left to a reset loop that doesn't exist.
*set {(aag_p & "can_retreat")} false
*set {(aag_p & "flee_hp_pct")} 0

*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
*comment FIGHTS
*comment fight_library is the plug-in entry: a scene names library types and calls it. The named fights after it are
*comment thin callers that say who is in the fight and nothing else. Nothing is rewritten per fight.
*comment ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

*label fight_library
*comment THE PLUG-IN ENTRY. A scene that needs a fight, and has no fight_<name> label of its own, sets
*comment   combat_lib_1 (and optionally combat_lib_2 through combat_lib_6): a library type id for each slot, and
*comment   combat_is_nonlethal true if the brawl should end in surrender rather than death (default false),
*comment   plus, if it wants its own names, combat_enemy_name_override and combat_enemy_epithet_override for slot 1
*comment   and the combat_enemy2_ / _3_ / _4_ / _5_ / _6_ versions for the others,
*comment then *gosub_scene combat fight_library and reads combat_outcome afterwards like any fight. Call anything
*comment humanoid by naming it, with no other change: a black knight with a sword and a bandit archer is
*comment   *set combat_lib_1 "swordsman"   *set combat_enemy_name_override "Black Knight"
*comment   *set combat_enemy_epithet_override "the black knight"
*comment   *set combat_lib_2 "archer"      *set combat_enemy2_name_override "Bandit Archer"
*comment   *set combat_enemy2_epithet_override "the bandit archer"
*comment (a proper name works too: epithet "Captain Vane"). To make one fighter tougher or weaker than its type, set any
*comment of combat_enemy_hp_override, combat_enemy_ac_override and combat_enemy_atk_override (hit points, the number the
*comment player must beat to hit it, and its to-hit bonus; combat_enemy2_ through combat_enemy6_ for the other slots). Leave one
*comment alone and that number stays the type's own. Captain Vane, a swordsman with 24 HP, AC 14 and +5 to hit:
*comment   *set combat_enemy_hp_override 24   *set combat_enemy_ac_override 14   *set combat_enemy_atk_override 5
*comment The number of enemies is the number of combat_lib_ slots filled. Slot 1 decides the player's lines: creatures
*comment (lacedon, mire_rat, beast) use apply_creature_weapon_flavor, everything else apply_generic_weapon_flavor; both
*comment switch on the weapon's damage type. No prose is written per fight.
*comment Human types by weapon: swordsman, axeman, knifeman, maceman, spearman, hookman (hooked pole), archer, slinger,
*comment and guard (plain mace, no special move). Creatures: lacedon, mire_rat, and beast, a generic natural-attack monster
*comment (wolf, boar, bear, giant spider, ghoul) that becomes whatever you name it:
*comment   *set combat_lib_1 "beast"   *set combat_enemy_name_override "Dire Wolf"   *set combat_enemy_epithet_override "the dire wolf"
*comment   *set combat_enemy_hp_override 14   *set combat_enemy_atk_override 4
*comment combat_lib_* is cleared by fight_cleanup.
*set combat_enemy_count 1
*set combat_apply_slot 1
*set combat_apply_type combat_lib_1
*gosub apply_enemy_type
*comment Slot 1 is mandatory; slots 2 and beyond are however many combat_lib_N the caller filled (up to
*comment combat_enemy_slot_cap), checked via a bounded loop and indirection instead of one hand-written *if per
*comment slot. Widening the slot cap needs no change here.
*temp flb_i 2
*label fight_library_slot_loop
*if (flb_i <= combat_enemy_slot_cap)
  *temp flb_lib {("combat_lib_" & flb_i)}
  *if (not(flb_lib = ""))
    *set combat_enemy_count flb_i
    *set combat_apply_slot flb_i
    *set combat_apply_type flb_lib
    *gosub apply_enemy_type
  *set flb_i + 1
  *goto fight_library_slot_loop
*set combat_round 1
*if ((combat_lib_1 = "lacedon") or ((combat_lib_1 = "mire_rat") or (combat_lib_1 = "beast")))
  *gosub apply_creature_weapon_flavor
*else
  *gosub apply_generic_weapon_flavor
*gosub apply_ally_roster
*gosub fight_setup
*goto fight_round_hub

*comment The named fights below are thin callers of fight_library: they say who is in the fight and nothing else.

*comment Range (3) instead of the type-library default of Engaged, to play with the range-tier system: watch it
*comment close one tier a turn on its own, and Push Forward through Close and Mid-Range before it's in melee reach.

*label fight_dev_ally_ranged_test
*comment Dev-hub only (dev_hub.txt dev_combat_test_hub) -- no story scene calls this. One lacedon, plus one
*comment ranged ally (the "archer" library type, renamed "Kess" via the override fields), to watch
*comment resolve_ally_attack in action: does it hit, does it roll damage, does it help drop the enemy. Also the
*comment first real caller of apply_ally_roster/apply_ally_type -- was hand-set stats before the ally type
*comment library existed (2026-09-28).
*comment Range (2026-09-29): explicitly Close (1), never left at the combat_ally1_range *create default of 0 --
*comment apply_allytype_archer, like apply_type_archer on the enemy side, never sets range itself, so a fight
*comment that doesn't set it is implicitly claiming its archer is standing in melee. Every real enemy-side ranged
*comment fight (fight_wreckers, fight_shroud_thieves, fight_dredge_ambush) already follows this same rule
*comment explicitly, for the same reason: since the close-range disadvantage penalty (§4.4, resolve_ally_attack)
*comment now reads this field, leaving it at 0 would falsely show Kess fighting at disadvantage on a shot that
*comment was always meant to be clean, in every fight that ever recruits her -- caught live via scripted_play.js
*comment before this fixture, and every ranged-ally fixture, would have silently shipped that regression.

*label fight_dev_reach_test
*comment Dev-hub only (dev_hub.txt dev_combat_test_hub) -- no story scene calls this. THE test for the
*comment close-combat ranged penalty and the three spell reach tags (tools/spell_catalog.json, spell_range_of).
*comment Two enemies, one Engaged and one at Long Range, so a single page shows BOTH sides of every rule at once.
*comment
*comment What to check, as the fight runs (a caster with a ranged cantrip and a touch cantrip is the useful
*comment character here -- Cadre Force-Weaver has Fire Bolt and Shocking Grasp):
*comment   Engaged + ranged spell  -> the roll banner reads "(Disadvantage - in reach)"
*comment   Long Range + ranged spell -> no such banner
*comment   Engaged + melee weapon or touch spell -> never penalised, at any range
*comment   touch spell (Shocking Grasp) -> OFFERED only while a target is Engaged. It must VANISH from the menu
*comment     at Long Range rather than appear at a penalty -- that is the reach question, distinct from the
*comment     disadvantage question, and confusing the two is the bug this probe exists to catch.
*comment   melee weapon at Long Range -> not offered at all (the existing "needs range 0 to swing" gate)
*comment KNOWN LIMIT, unchanged: a RANGED ALLY still takes no penalty at all. resolve_ally_attack has no
*comment advantage/disadvantage concept yet (see its own header), and the per-pair distance an ally would need
*comment does not exist in this model. That path is unwired on purpose, not forgotten -- see GAMEPLAY_MECHANICS_RULES.
*set combat_is_nonlethal false
*set combat_lib_1 "lacedon"
*set combat_enemy_name_override "Silt-Lurker Lacedon"
*set combat_lib_2 "lacedon"
*set combat_enemy2_name_override "Second Silt-Lurker"
*set combat_enemy_range 0
*set combat_enemy2_range 3
*goto fight_library

*label fight_dev_squad_test
*comment Dev-hub only (dev_hub.txt dev_combat_test_hub) -- no story scene calls this. Two enemies and two allies
*comment together, each opened at a different range, to play-test the nearest-enemy movement targeting
*comment (fight_pick_nearest_target), kill-priority attack targeting (fight_pick_target), free movement/Dash, and
*comment ally AI all at once instead of in isolation. The Vanguard Swordsman (Close, 1) is nearer than the
*comment Rearguard Archer (Long Range, 3) -- Push Forward/Fall Back should NARRATE against whichever of the two is
*comment actually nearest for that direction rather than whichever has less HP, and the button text should preview
*comment that same tier pair ("Close -> Engaged"). **Watch the two ranges after a single Push Forward:** this pair is
*comment also the check for combat_shift_all_ranges, which moves EVERY living enemy, so they must go 0 and 2. A 0 and
*comment 3 result means the tier change went to a single slot again -- the pre-2026-09-28 behaviour, and the reason
*comment this probe's original note only claimed to test "movement targeting." Rook (melee ally, Mid-Range, 2) has to
*comment close on its own target over a couple of rounds; Kess (ranged ally) attacks every turn regardless of range,
*comment same as fight_dev_ally_ranged_test.

*set combat_enemy2_epithet_override "the rearguard archer"
*set combat_enemy2_range 3
*set combat_ally_lib_1 "guard"
*set combat_ally1_name_override "Rook"
*set combat_ally1_epithet_override "Rook"
*set combat_ally1_range 2
*set combat_ally_lib_2 "archer"
*set combat_ally2_name_override "Kess"
*set combat_ally2_epithet_override "Kess"
*comment Range: explicitly Close (1), same reason as fight_dev_ally_ranged_test's own comment -- left at the

*label fight_dredge_ambush
*comment Night ambush in Dredge-End: one bruiser (weapon picked at random so repeat ambushes do not all read as the
*comment same mace swing) plus a weak slinger. Balance-simmed at level 1 against the ten stock builds: ~64% overall win
*comment rate, on par with the Silt-Lurker -- casters are the ones at risk, which is why the calling scene offers a
*comment toll / slip-away route instead of forcing this fight. Lethal fight, reports combat_outcome like any other;
*comment the CALLER handles "rescued" (robbed, 1 HP) and "player_died" per the fight_player_died contract.
*set combat_is_nonlethal false
*comment Shares combat_roll_initiative's own whole-fight lock (combat_init_locked/locked_combat_init_page_id,
*comment this file, 2026-09-30) -- this roll happens before *goto fight_library even reaches fight_setup, but on
*comment the same first page as everything fight_setup itself locks with that flag, so the same guard covers it.
*comment Unlocked, a same-page refresh picked a DIFFERENT bruiser weapon type entirely (not just a different roll
*comment of the SAME type's ability scores, which apply_ability_score_range's own lock already covers) -- each
*comment type carries its own atk_bonus/dmg_bonus, so this alone reproduced as a drifting combat_enemy_atk_bonus/
*comment dmg_bonus even after that fix.
*if (not(combat_init_locked)) or (not(locked_combat_init_page_id = page_id))
  *temp da_pick 0
  *rand da_pick 1 4
  *if (da_pick = 1)
    *set combat_lib_1 "maceman"
  *if (da_pick = 2)
    *set combat_lib_1 "knifeman"
  *if (da_pick = 3)
    *set combat_lib_1 "hookman"
  *if (da_pick = 4)
    *set combat_lib_1 "axeman"
  *comment Remembered for the caller: the slots shift as foes fall, so the bruiser cannot be read back afterwards.
  *comment MUST be captured here, inside the lock, not as a later unconditional line -- combat_lib_1 itself is a
  *comment transient, CONSUMED value (fight_library/apply_enemy_type clear it once read), so a locked replay that
  *comment skips the roll above would otherwise copy whatever combat_lib_1 had already been reset to by the END of
  *comment the original run (empty), not the type it actually rolled (found live: dredge_ambush_bruiser came back
  *comment "" on refresh instead of the real pick).
  *set dredge_ambush_bruiser combat_lib_1
*set combat_lib_2 "slinger"
*comment Range: the slinger opens at Close (1) instead of the type-library default of Engaged (0) -- it keeps
*comment its distance, matching its own flavor, while staying only one Push Forward action away so a melee-only
*comment build isn't shut out for more than a turn. Close over Medium deliberately: this and the other two
*comment fights sharing this comment are balance-simmed at ~64% overall win already, and Medium would cost a
*comment melee build an extra turn of exposure the sim never accounted for. The bruiser slot (1) stays at the
*comment default 0, engaged from turn one.
*set combat_enemy2_range 1
*goto fight_library
